## The ladder — one dot per item
🔴 **Not built** → 🟡 **Built, pending review** (branch/preview, unapproved) → 🟣 **Approved on preview, not live** → 🩷 **Live, NOT verified** (shipped, nobody's walked it) → 🟢 **Live + VERIFIED** (walked, works on real data). ⏸ = a 🔴 blocked on something.
**Iron rule: nothing is 🟢 until walked live in production.** Numbers are **stable IDs, not sequence** (links from other docs depend on them). Owner = who owns the next step: 🧍 founder · 🤖 Claude · 🤝 both · — none.

> **🧭 FOUR-DOC CONTRACT:** **LAUNCH-PAD** = daily execution · **PRODUCT-INVENTORY** *(this doc)* = the only product-status board · **KIND-MASTER** = strategy/decisions/log · **V2-TRACKER** = future detail. Status truth lives here, nowhere else. **Money model of record → [`CASHFLOW-LAB.html`](./CASHFLOW-LAB.html)** (#556). **`BUILD-STATUS.md` was a fifth status doc and is RETIRED** → `archive/BUILD-STATUS-26JUL.md` (#555) — it claimed *"nothing left"* while the whole sending spine was 🔴.

> **A client can pay and we cannot deliver them.** `figsy.ts:26` is `const FROM = COLD_FROM` — one shared sending address for every client — `client_inboxes` is read by **no send path**, and **no SMTP client is installed**. That is **#211**, 🔴 since 30 Jun, now broken into **#547–#553** in the **📮 THE SENDING SPINE** section below. Everything else on this board is either live or not on the critical path. **The MBF demo (#544) is sellable today; delivery is not.**

## 🧭 THE NEW MAP — read this first (Milla&Vida pivot, founder-locked 22 Jul)
The company now trades as **Milla&Vida** (K.I.N.D stays the registered company). The product is **one engine, two portals** — so read every item below through these homes:

| Home | What it is | Old name it replaces |
|------|-----------|----------------------|
| **⚙️ FIGSY — THE ENGINE** | The AI that does the heavy lifting: find → reveal → write → send → reply → book. **No longer sold as a product** — it powers the two portals. | *(was "FIGSY, the SDR we sell")* |
| **🖥 VIDA — OPERATOR CONSOLE** | Where **we** run a client end-to-end (ICP → source → draft → approve → send → triage → book). The **admin / "nervous system" folds in here** as Vida's top-right dropdown. | *(was "Vida, inbound Host chatbot" — superseded)* |
| **💜 MILLA — CLIENT PORTAL** | What the **client** sees: masked leads, 👍/✕ approve-to-pursue, concierge chat, meetings, reports. Reviews only — never drives. | *(was "Milla, the Brain agent" — superseded)* |
| **🧠 NEXUS** | Each client's **private learning brain** — learns on that client's results only, never shared. | *(was "one shared brain across agents")* |
| **🌐 WEBSITE** | The Milla&Vida marketing site (re-skin of the current framework). | — |

**Money:** $4 charged **only** on the client's 👍 approve ($1 reveal + $3 work fire after; reviewing free; dead email auto-refunded; prepaid credits, never chased). **Glide path:** managed → co-pilot → self-serve (only *who clicks approve* changes). **Build order:** Website → Vida → Milla (see LAUNCH-PAD 14-day map). Design references: `docs/mv-previews/`.

**Nothing was deleted or renumbered in this reorg** — sections were re-headed to the homes above, the 14 new build items (#477–#490) were minted, and retired old-agent *scope* is noted, not removed. Deeper physical re-sorting of legacy rows is a later pass; the map + headers carry the clarity now.

| 491 | 🩷 | **[VIDA · post-14-days] Per-cron failure alerting** — audit gap: all 25 cron jobs die silently if an env var is unset (one blanket alert only); surface per-job failure state in Vida's ops view. Not launch-blocking — the operator watches the loop daily meanwhile. **29 Jul — BUILT (`lib/cron-health.ts`, pure + 25 tests).** `callInternal` already wrote every run to `cron_runs` and dead-lettered every failure, and **neither is a signal** — both are PULL surfaces, true when written and read only when somebody thinks to look. **Two detectors, because a job stops in two different ways:** ① it RAN AND FAILED → a founder alert **naming the job**, and ② it **NEVER RAN AT ALL**, which leaves no row for ① to fire on — the **claim-then-crash path #343 introduced** (a process claims the slot, dies before finishing, every other replica correctly stands down, and the job silently does not happen), also what a dead scheduler or a stray `RUN_CRONS=false` looks like. **The hard part was the throttle, not the alert:** the send job runs every 2h, so alerting per failure sends twelve identical emails a day and trains the founder to filter the channel — which is how the next real one is missed. Three rules held at once: a **new** failure after a success always alerts · an **ongoing** outage stays quiet 24h · but it **nags again** after 24h so silence is never mistaken for recovery. **No new infra** — the throttle marker rides in `cron_runs.note` (a restart-proof record; an in-memory counter would reset on every deploy, exactly when a cron is most likely broken). The stale detector **measures each job's own median gap** rather than carrying a hardcoded daily-job list, so the four genuinely weekly jobs (`/digest/weekly`, `/cro/weekly-digest`, `/cmo/self-outreach`, `/data-moat/aggregate`) exclude themselves and an hourly job that stops is caught with no configuration. Runs inside the existing daily prune job — a new timer is one more thing that can die silently. **A history read failure ALERTS rather than staying quiet.** Red proven by removing the throttle and the weekly exclusion (3 fail). **29 Jul — VERIFIED WIRED, not just a lib nothing calls.** `cron.ts:82/99` imports and calls `shouldAlertFailure` + `failureAlertLines`, and `cron.ts:124/135` calls `staleJobs` + `staleAlertLines`. Both detectors are live in the runner, so the 25 unit tests are guarding code that actually executes. Flipped 🩷 — unverified in prod only because no cron has failed since it shipped. | 🤖 |

This was the money model being used for every session above while its own row read 'not built'.)* **[ENGINE] ONE WALLET / WORK MODEL (founder-locked 24 Jul — supersedes the 23-Jul re-time)** — a single dollar wallet per client; the client's 👍 charges a flat **$4 per approved lead, FINAL** (no $1/$3 split shown, no hold, no capture-on-booking, no release, no TTL, no hand-backs). # ░ 🚀 THE LAUNCH PATH (25 Jul flow walk — founder-locked · #520–#537) ░
*The walk found the shell was built and the FEATURES were not. Everything the old self-serve console could do — build an ICP by conversation, generate people, assign them, suggest a campaign, edit a sequence, send a test, hit run — sat behind `requireAuth` (a **client** JWT). Vida proxies with an admin key and NO client session, so the operator could look at a campaign and never propose, fill, preview, test or run one. These are the operator twins plus the client's half. **No new SQL** — every item uses tables that already exist, so none of it was blocked on the Supabase lockout. Historical detail: `docs/archive/BUILD-STATUS-26JUL.md` §THE LAUNCH PATH (**archived 26 Jul, #555 — read it as record, never as status**).*

| # | Dot | Item | Owner |
|---|-----|------|-------|

| 521 | 🩷 | *(🔴→🟡→🩷 25 Jul: shipped PR #1151.)* **[VIDA · V1] The onboarding checklist IS the setup guide** — each gap is a door: click it and land on the surface that closes it, instead of reading a label. | 🤖 |

Also refuses a non-active campaign instead of quietly doing nothing. | 🤖 |

Needs **two founder decisions before any build**: ① an SMS provider (none contracted — Twilio/Vonage cost + a SA sender ID) and ② the security call on link-only login (token TTL, single-use, device binding). Not launch-blocking: email+password works today. | 🤝 |
| 546 | 🩷 | *(🔴→🩷 26 Jul: shipped PR #1161.)* **[AUDIT] Both consoles told the truth about themselves** — a full audit found the product contradicting itself in front of a paying client. Milla's chat header was a **hardcoded "● Campaign live"** with no condition on it, sitting inches from the KPI that correctly read *"Dormant"*; the lead-desk footer quoted **"$4 per approved lead"** while the button above it said *"included"*; the greeting quoted a flat $4 to clients holding 100 free approvals; the pack counter **never refreshed after approving**, so 20 approvals still read "100 included"; a 402 on a 20-lead batch said *"you need $4"* when it needed $80; and a partial batch failure cleared every selection, losing the picks still to retry. In Vida, the header's **"N to triage" counted replies that arrived TODAY** while the client list counted replies still open at any age — so it read "0 to triage" beside a client showing "152 replies to handle" — and it counted demos and house accounts, putting our own test noise in the operator's headline numbers. | 🤖 |
| 545 | 🩷 | *(🔴→🩷 26 Jul: shipped PR #1161.)* **[MILLA] The client's own messages reach an operator** — Milla's chat **mutates nothing** (verified: zero `.update()`/`.rpc()` calls) and `/operator/asks` only surfaced threads **we** started, so a client typing *"pause my campaign"* landed in `milla_messages` where **nobody ever looked**. On a managed service that is the client's one channel. A client message now pages the operator (throttled to one per client per 15 min) and appears **first** in Vida → Asks under *"they said this to Milla — she can't action it, you can"*. The chat chips were also promising actions the chat cannot perform; they now read as requests, which is what they actually are. | 🤖 |
| 544 | 🩷 | *(🔴→🩷 26 Jul: shipped PR #1160/#1161.)* **[DEMO] MBF — the demo account, one fixed cast** — *"5 demos = 1 sale"*, so the stage must never move. 40 invented people, score-ordered and fixed: 12 already being worked, **22 waiting to be picked** (≥20 on purpose, so the minimum-20 gate can be demonstrated rather than adapting down), 6 passed who are visibly worse fits. Planted history — enrolments, 24 sends with realistic opens, $0 pack ledger rows that agree with the on-screen counter, two meetings still ahead, outcome events. **No randomness anywhere**, unlike `seed-company.ts`: a demo that reshuffles cannot be rehearsed. Reset is wipe-then-seed, so the first build and the between-demos reset run identical code. **Cannot send:** `is_demo` is a hard stop inside the send path, every address is `.invalid` (RFC 2606), sourcing is pool-only at $0, and the cold-check skips demos. One button in Vida → Engine. **29 Jul — the transactional BACKSTOP now covers `.invalid` too.** `email.ts isRealRecipient` describes itself as *"the last line of defence: any transactional send to a non-deliverable address is dropped before it hits Resend"* — and it filtered `.internal`, `kind-demo.`, `example.`, `.test` and `localhost` while **missing `.invalid`, the one TLD every MBF address actually carries**. It never bit, because the `is_demo` stop fires earlier on every path; that is precisely the reason to fix it rather than shrug — **a backstop that only holds while the thing in front of it holds is not a backstop**, and this one exists to work when it is the ONLY line. A demo address reaching Resend is a hard bounce on the sending domain every real client shares. Matched on the domain suffix, not a substring, so `invalidations@acme.com` is still a real mailbox. Pinned against `MBF_MARKER` so renaming the demo domain fails the test rather than silently reopening the hole. 11 tests (the function had **none** — the only prior reference mocked it), red proven (5 fail without the line). **Dot unchanged at 🩷:** the demo is already live and this adds a guard, so `flip-dots.sh` correctly reported *0 flipped*. | 🤖 |
| 543 | 🩷 | *(🔴→🩷 26 Jul: shipped PR #1159.)* **[MONEY] Cold clients — 30 days with no approvals suspends them** — *"we're not a free service."* Their sender costs us ~$40/month from the day they sign and keeps costing whether they approve anyone or not. A nightly check pauses the campaigns of anyone 30 days idle, warned a week out. **Caught while wiring it:** suspending pauses campaigns and `approveLead` **fail-closes without an active campaign**, so a suspended client would have been locked out of the only action that un-suspends them — approving now resumes a paused campaign on their own click, no operator needed, and only `paused` is touched (never `draft` or `completed`). Never-approved is deliberately **not** cold (that client hasn't started — a different chase), and an unparseable date can never read as idle. Vida shows Suspended / Going-quiet. | 🤖 |
| 542 | 🩷 | *(🔴→🩷 26 Jul: shipped PR #1159.)* **[MONEY] The minimum-20 gate** — the cashflow model made it concrete: a client's inbox costs ~$40/month from day one, so at $4 a lead the first **~13 approvals** buy nothing but their own sender, and a client who approves five is one we run a free mail service for. They now choose **at least 20** the first time round. **Server-enforced on every door into the money path** — `/approve`, `/reveal` and the new `/approve-batch` — because a disabled button is a suggestion anyone can step around with a fetch. Asks for `min(20, what they actually have)` so a client we only sent 12 people isn't given an impossible gate, and **releases entirely at 20 approvals ever**: it starts the relationship, it doesn't nag someone already working with us. Milla's desk becomes pick-then-start with a bar counting them to the number. | 🤖 |
| 541 | 🩷 | *(🔴→🟡→🩷 26 Jul: shipped PR #1159, live. ⚠️ **PRICE SUPERSEDED 3 Aug: the pack is now $299, not $99** — see #609. The mechanism this row describes is unchanged and still live; only the number moved.)* **[MONEY] The $99 onboarding pack — 100 leads included, then $4** (founder-locked 25 Jul, after walking the real cost: 200 records at $0.28 = $56 · 100 worked at ~$0.06 = $6 · Stripe $3.17 · inbox month 1 $4.50 ≈ **$70, keeping ~$29**). Built as a **counted quota, not a wallet credit** — `lib/onboarding-pack.ts` (16 tests) derives it from rows that already exist (a purchase row; leads with `revealed_at`), so no column to sync and no fiction in the balance. First 100 approvals charge nothing; the 101st charges $4 as before. **Two money bugs caught while wiring it:** a dead email on a pack approval would have **credited $4 the client never paid** (now hands the pack slot back instead), and the result claimed `charged: true` on a free approval (now tells the truth, and Milla's card says *"Included in your 100"*). Milla's hero KPI shows **leads left**, not a $0 wallet. **Also: no time limit on paid leads** — the 72h TTL never expired anything, it just made surfaced leads vanish off the client's desk with no notice, which would have silently eaten most of a 100-lead pack. | 🤖 |
| 540 | 🩷 | *(🔴→🟡→🩷 26 Jul: shipped PR #1159, live.)* **[VIDA v2] The console opens on the step, not the objects** — Vida had **eight tabs for a five-action job**, named after objects (People · Campaign · ICP · Sequence · Asks), so every screen asked the operator to work out where they were. New `GET /operator/worklist` (one query per TABLE across all clients, never one per client) + `lib/client-step.ts` — a **pure, unit-tested decision table** (17 tests) returning the client's step, the one next action, who it's waiting on, and an urgency for sorting. # ░ 📮 THE SENDING SPINE — the reason we cannot onboard a paying client (26 Jul · #547–#560) ░
*Minted 26 Jul after the founder pushed back on "nothing is stopping you". **#211 is the parent** and has been 🔴 since 30 Jun; these are its parts, each small enough to be built and verified on its own. Every claim below was read in the code this session, not remembered.*

**The finding, in one paragraph.** `apps/api/src/lib/figsy.ts:26` is `const FROM = COLD_FROM` — a **module-level constant**, used by both `resend.emails.send` calls (≈707 and ≈1096), so every client on the platform emails from the same address. `client_inboxes` exists (`apps/api/src/migrations/20260725_client_inboxes.sql`) and Vida's assign route does **one insert** into it, but the table is **read nowhere on any send path** — only by `demo-mbf.ts`, `pending-migrations.ts` and `routes/operator.ts`. There is **no SMTP client in the dependency tree** (no nodemailer, no smtp library): Resend is the only transport and it sends only from **our** verified domain. `lib/smartlead.ts` is a key-verification stub. Instantly is not integrated. Replies all arrive through **one** Resend inbound webhook. So *"sends on your own warmed inbox"* — promised on the website, in `mv-previews/home.html`, in `flow-vida.html` and in the pitch — **does not exist**, and a client who pays cannot be delivered.

| # | Dot | Item | Owner |
|---|-----|------|-------|
| 547 | 🩷 | *(🔴→🟡 26 Jul: built, on PR.)* **[ENGINE · A1] The send path resolves the account's own inbox — or does not send** — the `FROM` module constant is **gone**; both cold-send call sites now resolve the client's own mailbox off `client_inboxes` via `lib/sending-inbox.ts` (a pure, unit-tested decision function — 18 tests — so the ordering rules and the refusal paths can be proved without a database). **No inbox = no send:** the step is rolled back to DUE, the founder is alerted, and the reason is recorded. **There is no fallback left to fall back TO** — `COLD_FROM` is no longer imported by the cold path at all. **Two rules the tests pin:** a `warming` branded mailbox never sends (sending on it is what un-warms it), so during the pooled→branded overlap the **pooled** one sends; and once the branded one is `active` it wins — but a half-configured preferred mailbox is skipped rather than taking the client off the air. **29 Jul — THE LAST SHARED SENDER TO A REAL PROSPECT IS CLOSED.** The sweep that shipped this left `lib/manual-reply.ts:54` sending from `COLD_FROM`, so after #547 the automated steps left the client's mailbox and a **human reply in the same conversation left the shared address** — the thread changed sender mid-conversation, and every client's manual replies pooled their complaints onto one domain. It now runs the same resolve-or-refuse as the sequence path, refusing with a **409 and the operator-readable reason** (a human clicked Send, so the reason goes to the screen, not just a log); a mail-server rejection returns **502** instead of the old `throw`, which surfaced as a bare 500 with no way to tell whether the mail had left. `COLD_REPLY_TO` is deliberately gone with it: pointing replies at our shared address would send the prospect's next message to **us** rather than the mailbox holding the conversation, and would defeat #551's inbox→client routing. 20 tests, **red proven** by simulating the fallback (8 fail, including *"NEVER falls back to the shared address"*). **DECIDED the same session — the CONSENT email stays OURS, not per-client** (`lib/email.ts`, reasoning written into the code): it is our mail sent as the processor ("on behalf of X via K.I.N.D"), it carries the POPIA audit trail and our own consent/opt-out links, and — the deciding reason — it is the one message that goes out **before consent exists**, so putting it on a client's freshly-warmed branded mailbox is exactly what un-warms it, and it fires at sourcing time when a client may have no mailbox at all. 12.2 is not breached: the sender is us, identified as us. ⚠️ **Left open on purpose:** consent mail sends from the **transactional** identity while D4 says cold-adjacent mail must not poison that domain — moving it to `FIGSY_COLD_FROM` affects the domain every invoice and password reset leaves from, so it needs its own PR and the founder's call. Parent: #211. **⚠️ RECONCILED 6 Aug (#628) — BUILT, and LAUNCH-PAD's BLOCK A had gone on describing it as unbuilt for days.** The shared `const FROM = COLD_FROM` is gone: the sender resolves per client from `client_inboxes` (`lib/sending-inbox.ts`) and goes out over that mailbox's own SMTP (`lib/mailer.ts`), with **deliberately nothing to fall back to** when a client has no mailbox. Proof rides send-day (A14). Dot NOT flipped here — status is this doc's to hold, and a doc-repair is not the place to move seven of them; raised for the founder in the #628 PR. | 🤖 |
| 548 | 🩷 | *(🔴→🟡 26 Jul: **founder chose option B** — built, on PR.)* **[ENGINE · A2] A transport that can send AS a client mailbox** — the physical gap behind #547: Resend authenticates **our** domain and no SMTP client was installed, so the product could not speak to any other mailbox. **Founder-locked B (we press send, through the mailbox the provider warmed) over A (hand the lead to the provider's campaign engine).** The deciding argument was the founder's: the sending brain is **already built and tested** — sequence engine, send window, daily caps, A/B subjects, kill-switch, reply classifier — and A would have put a second brain in charge of *when* an email goes out. B changes the `From:` and the connection, nothing else. It also earns what A never could: a client who says *"use my own Google Workspace"* is a client A must turn away. Built as `lib/mailer.ts` (nodemailer, per-send transport — a pooled connection shared across clients could send one client's mail down another's authenticated session) returning the **same verdict shape as `interpretSend`**, so every existing #338 rollback path works byte-for-byte. B's cost is one mailbox password per client → `lib/inbox-secret.ts`, AES-256-GCM, fresh IV per encryption, authenticated so tampering throws instead of handing garbage to a mail server (25 tests). **🧍 Founder owes: `INBOX_SECRET_KEY` on @kind/api (`openssl rand -hex 32`) — no key = no send, by design** — plus one mailbox's SMTP host/port/user/password. ⚠️ Google and Microsoft mailboxes block plain SMTP by default (Google needs an App Password, Microsoft needs SMTP AUTH enabled per mailbox); Zapmail/SmartSenders sells them for outreach so it likely supplies working details. Parent: #211. | 🤝 |

Parked until a client is in the works, which is the right call: it buys nothing before then. | 🤝 | ✅ **A22's 🤖 HALF BUILT 13 Aug (R25) — and the build found the gap that would have defeated the ruling.** Verified first that the month-one flow ALREADY existed and was wired (`pushApprovedLeadToSmartlead` at `approve-lead.ts:443`; the fail-closed gate in `smartlead-map.ts`; pooled/branded assignment at `/operator/inboxes/assign` + `/brand`; **the ~day-29 switch already data-driven** in `sending-inbox.ts`, branded-active outranking pooled) — so nothing was rebuilt. ⚠️ **THE GAP:** with no key the push refuses `no_api_key`, and that refusal is deliberately NOT alerted (it would fire on every approval and train the founder to ignore it) — so those leads were **charged, revealed, enrolled and never handed to Smartlead, with nothing recording the skip and nothing replaying it.** On unlock day every lead approved beforehand would have stayed un-pushed forever: *"day 1 works"* true only for leads approved after the purchase. **`lib/smartlead-backfill.ts` is the recovery** — query-driven off `revealed_at` (never a second copy of "approved" that can drift), bounded, operator-triggered at `/operator/smartlead/backfill`, calling the SAME push so every gate re-runs per lead; halts with ONE reason when the key is missing or the kill-switch is off; **"0 of 40 pushed" reads as a warning, never as success.** Also: the System row's superseded 26-Jul trigger replaced with R25 + a **401-is-the-plan-not-the-key** diagnosis, a PDL-tier row (R26) that refuses to spend to prove a tier, a double-assign guard that returns a readable 409 instead of a raw 500, and **`docs/UNLOCK-DAY-RUNBOOK.md`**. 14 pins, red-proved 3 ways. **Nothing turns on: the kill-switch stays off and the key still 401s.** | 🤖 |
| 551 | 🩷 | **[ENGINE · A5] Replies land back against the right inbox** — every reply today arrives through **one** Resend inbound webhook. The moment clients send from their own mailboxes, replies arrive at **those** mailboxes, so without per-provider ingestion mapped inbox → client → lead → thread the unibox goes silent for every paying client — and we miss the meeting we already charged for. Must be idempotent on provider message id (the same discipline as the Stripe webhook's UNIQUE reference). Parent: #211. **BUILT 27 Jul (PR pending).** The chain is now **inbox → client → lead → thread**: `InboundReply` carries `toEmail`, `resolveInboxOwner()` maps `client_inboxes.email` → client, and `routeReply()` keeps ONLY that client's matches. **The defect does not bite until the first client sends from their own mailbox — which is exactly why it had to be built before that, not after.** Today every reply lands at one shared Resend inbox, so matching on the prospect's address and fanning out to every client holding that lead is CORRECT (R1); the moment a client has their own mailbox the fan-out becomes **one client reading another client's inbound mail**. **An unknown inbox falls back to today's fan-out** — a shared inbox, a provider that omits `to`, a mailbox not yet recorded — because unknown must never mean dropped, and that fallback is what makes this safe to ship before anyone has a mailbox. **A known inbox with no matching lead returns EMPTY and alerts** rather than falling back, because falling back IS the harm. The status filter is deliberately absent: a released mailbox still receives mail for weeks and the pooled→branded handover overlaps by design, so filtering to active would orphan the tail of every switched-over client. **Idempotency now keys on the PROVIDER MESSAGE ID, namespaced** — `svix-id` is a Resend transport id, so with a second provider the guard would key on an empty string, fail open on every event, and a retried hot reply would re-run the CRM deal, the alert and the counters. 25 tests, red proven by breaking the fallback and the namespace. **29 Jul — THE SMARTLEAD FEEDER IS BUILT, and building it required extracting the pipeline.** The spine (`reply-ingest.ts`) was already provider-agnostic, but the **256 lines that ACT on its judgements** — store the reply, classify once, suppress opt-outs, escalate risky replies, run the hot path, push to CRM — still sat **inside the Resend Express handler**. So adding a second feeder had exactly two options: duplicate them, or move them. Duplicating is what #589 exists to forbid (*"each of the three providers grows its own copy of the same five bugs"*), and **R7 alone — a failed blocklist write meaning we keep emailing someone who said stop — is not a thing to own two copies of**. They now live in **`lib/reply-pipeline.ts`**, shared. **It is a MOVE, not a rewrite:** four mechanical changes (the four `res.json(); return` exits became returned results; `fromEmail`/`body`/`subject` read off `inbound`, which already carried them; `payload`→`ctx.rawPayload`; the Resend-only body-fetch diagnostics→`ctx`). **Proof it changed nothing:** all 59 existing reply tests stayed green, including `reply-fanout.route.test.ts`, which drives the real Express handler — only **two source-scan assertions** failed, both because they read `routes/figsy.ts` for calls that had moved; repointed at the pipeline, and a new one added asserting the route **DELEGATES** rather than re-inlining the loop. `figsy.ts` 3240→2999 lines. **The feeder itself:** `POST /figsy/replies/smartlead` — authenticate, dedup, parse, delegate. Routes by the **receiving mailbox**, so the same prospect replying to client A's mailbox and to client B's lands against A and B respectively; an unknown mailbox **fans out exactly as today** rather than dropping; a reply at a known mailbox from someone that client does not hold goes to **NEITHER** and alerts (falling back would post one client's mail into another's unibox). Non-reply events (SENT/OPEN) are skipped — processing our own outbound as a reply could classify our own email as hot. Dedup is **namespaced by provider** via `replyEventKey`, because two providers can issue the same message id. 15 route tests, red proven (6 fail). **⚠️ SHIPS INERT:** no `SMARTLEAD_WEBHOOK_SECRET` → 503, so merging changes nothing until the founder sets it and points Smartlead at the URL — the right default for a public endpoint that can suppress a client's leads. **⚠️ THE PAYLOAD SHAPE IS UNVERIFIED** (key 401s, docs 403 us): field names are inferred, isolated behind a tolerant alias list in ONE function, and an unreadable payload **alerts with the keys Smartlead actually sent** so it is corrected from evidence rather than another guess. **CHECK IT IN SMARTLEAD'S UI AFTER THE FIRST PUSH** — same instruction #550 carries. **Instantly (#549) deliberately NOT built** — blocked on the bill, and untestable. **30 Jul — THE INSTANTLY HALF IS DEAD, and that is the right outcome rather than a loss.** The planned Instantly reply feeder is **never needed**: our own engine sends our outreach from **our own mailboxes**, so replies arrive in **our own pipeline** — no webhook, no payload shape to guess, no third feeder to keep in step. The item's stated harm (*"the unibox goes silent for every paying client"*) was always about CLIENTS, and the **Smartlead feeder built in #1224 covers exactly that** and still ships inert until a client exists. **What this actually proves:** the provider-agnostic spine (#589) earned its keep in the other direction too — a provider we decided not to use cost us nothing to drop. **⚠️ RECONCILED 6 Aug (#628) — BUILT.** `lib/smartlead-inbound.ts` plus its route feed replies from a client's own mailbox into the unibox, routed on the **receiving mailbox** rather than on lead match alone; `routes/smartlead-inbound.route.test.ts` pins the case that matters — two clients emailing the same prospect must never see each other's replies. Proof rides A18. | 🤖 |
| 552 | 🩷 | *(🔴→🟡 26 Jul: built, on PR.)* **[VIDA · A6] Mailbox details go IN, and the board says who can actually send** — the founder hit this within a minute of the migration finishing: the Engine page had an **"Assign pooled inbox"** button and **nowhere to type the connection details**, so pressing it produced a client who read as covered on the board and still could not email anyone. Plus a red banner when `INBOX_SECRET_KEY` is unset, because that is a silent whole-platform outage otherwise. `normalisePort` is extracted and tested: **port and encryption mode cannot be decided apart** (465 implicit TLS / 587 STARTTLS), and `Number('')` → 0 reaching the transport is a 20-second hang on every send. **29 Jul — THE "START WORK REFUSES WITHOUT ONE" AMBIGUITY IS RESOLVED, AND THE ANSWER IS *NO*.** LAUNCH-PAD claimed Start-work refused without an inbox; `start-work.ts` gated on payment/ICP/user and **not** on a mailbox, with the only refusal firing at send time. A documented gate that does not exist is worse than either behaviour, so it is now decided and implemented: **warn loudly, do not block.** The two halves fail in opposite directions — sourcing spends OUR data budget to fill a desk the client's $99 already bought, and the mailbox is a separate purchase *we* make days later, so refusing there idles a paying client waiting on us; sending touches a real prospect, and that is where fail-closed already lives (`figsy.ts` refuses, rolls the step back, no fallback). `startWorkForClient` and `/operator/source` now carry a `sendWarning` / `send_warning`, and Vida renders it **amber — not green, not red**: green would bury it, red would read as "the sourcing failed", which is a different and wrong instruction. **It reuses `resolveSendingInbox` — the send path's own resolver — deliberately:** a hand-rolled *"does a `client_inboxes` row exist?"* check would drift the moment the send rules changed, and Vida would show a green light over a **warming** mailbox, one with **no SMTP credentials**, or a missing **`INBOX_SECRET_KEY`** — three refusal reasons, none of them "no row". **A failed check reports UNKNOWN, never a green light**, and never blocks. The `source_run` audit row records `cannot_send` so *"we sourced 200 for a client who could not send"* is answerable afterwards. 15 tests, red proven (10 fail when the warning is swallowed or a broken check green-lights). ⚠️ **Found while in the file, NOT fixed (own PR):** `/operator/source` reads active ICPs with `.eq('is_active', true).maybeSingle()` — the **#571 defect in a second place**; two active ICPs make PostgREST return an error, so `icp` is null and the route 400s *"No active ICP"* at a client who has two. **⚠️ RECONCILED 6 Aug (#628) — BUILT.** `sendReadiness()` in `lib/start-work.ts` backs the Vida **"Cannot send"** panel, and the panel deliberately never assumes the reason is "no mailbox". | 🤖 |

| 554 | 🩷 | **[SECURITY] RLS policies have never been audited** — seven migration files touch row-level security and **not one has been read end-to-end**. The API holds the service-role key so RLS is defence-in-depth rather than the primary guard (#261) — **but the anon key is public**, and #350 proved a real policy shipped as `{public} SELECT USING(true)`, making every visitor's IP and enrichment anon-readable in production. Application-level trust boundaries were verified clean across all 45 route files this cycle; **RLS is the one true breach risk left open.** Follow-on: #55a rep-level isolation. **DONE 27 Jul (PR pending) → [`docs/RLS-AUDIT.md`](./RLS-AUDIT.md).** Read **151 policies across 39 SQL files** — the row said *seven migration files*, so the surface is **5× what was claimed**. **#350 fixed one instance and nobody swept the class: this found FIVE MORE of the identical shape** — `lead_enrichment` (enrichment on real people), `partner_commissions` (what each partner is owed), `partner_referrals`, `partners`, `figsy_calls`, each `USING (true)` with **no `TO` clause, which in Postgres means PUBLIC**, each created by a **production-path** migration and dropped **nowhere** in that path (the only DROPs live in `staging-schema.sql`, a staging snapshot). Two rules produced every finding: a missing `TO` grants the table to the anon key **despite the policy being NAMED "Service role bypass"** — the name is the trap — and **permissive policies OR together**, so one `USING (true)` defeats every careful `current_client_id()` policy on the same table. Closed by `20260727_rls_close_public_policies` (drops POLICY DEFINITIONS only — no data, no columns; safe because the API uses the service-role key which bypasses RLS, and **no browser code reads any of those tables**, verified by reading every `.from()` in all three front ends). **The verdict is also LIVE:** `Vida → Engine → Run RLS audit` reads `pg_policies` + `pg_class.relrowsecurity` out of production, because with three migration dirs and two disagreeing snapshots (#558) a verdict read off a file is a verdict about a file — and a failed audit renders as an ERROR, never an empty pass. **✅ 6 Aug — ANSWERED FROM THE LIVE DATABASE FOR THE FIRST TIME, and the answer is clean.** A15 landed the session-pooler string, so `readLiveRls` could finally open a real Postgres connection and read `pg_catalog` instead of guessing from files. **Verdict, read at 09:55:14 via `aws-0-eu-west-1.pooler.supabase.com:5432`: "No exposed tables. 39 deny-all · 43 scoped · 82 tables read."** ⚠️ **This retires a standing claim in the docs.** This row and LAUNCH-PAD both carried *"the only true breach risk still open"* — written when nobody could see production and #350 had proved one policy shipped as `USING(true)`. Production now says nothing is exposed. The claim is answered, not deleted: it was true when written, and the thing that changed is that we can finally look. The same run also applied `20260727_rls_close_public_policies` and `20260727_rls_live_findings` (#554b — `client_inboxes` had **no RLS at all**), so part of the clean verdict is those migrations landing minutes earlier. **Still owed before 🟢:** a founder walk, per the iron rule — a green read is not a walked verdict. | 🤖 |

The other writer (`lib/vida.ts:187`, live for weeks) swallows its insert error, so if the column is absent it has been failing silently the whole time. Also: **`whatsapp_messages` and `subscribers`, two tables no migration creates at all**, and `opt_out_blocklist.whatsapp_number` — on the suppression table every send checks, which demonstrably works in production and which **no migration in this repo ever created**. **#558's own example traced:** four migrations redefine `credit_transactions_type_check` and disagree. **Only the last has a runner, and it is the one that DROPS `hold`/`release`** — which `20260724_one_wallet.sql` deliberately kept *"so old rows validate"*. `ADD CONSTRAINT` validates existing rows, so **if production holds one `type='hold'` row, pressing Run migrations throws.** Query 2 on the checklist answers it before the button is pressed. **⚠️ TWO BUGS IN MY OWN INSTRUMENT, recorded rather than quietly patched.** The scanner reported a table called `table` (from its own doc comment) and a column called `compat` (from the words *"Back-compat"* in a comment on `figsy.ts:1691`). The second was the serious one: the shared comment-stripper handled quotes and regex literals but **not nested template literals** — `figsy.ts:232` has a backtick inside a `${…}` inside a backtick, the outer template ended at the inner one, and **stripping stopped for the remaining 2,700 lines of the file.** The harmless half invents a finding; the dangerous half hides a real one — and fixing it is what surfaced `subscribers.source`. #561's env sweep uses the same stripper and was re-run: unchanged at 100. **AND THE SCAN STATES ITS OWN BLIND SPOT:** it reads **296 of 346** write calls — only inline object literals. The other 50 pass a variable, `leads` alone has 12, **including #599's own import**, so ✅ means *"nothing undeclared among the writes I can read"*. Found by red-proving, which is the only reason it is written down. **8 read-only queries on the founder checklist**, one per line, copy-pasteable into Vida → Engine — and a test asserts every fenced query on that page is a `SELECT`. **No writes to production in this PR, and #273 deliberately not started** — `schema.sql` still declares 10 of 68 tables, which is that item's job. 23 tests, red-proved six ways. | 🤝 **30 Jul (same day) — THE CHECKLIST POINTED AT A SCREEN THAT DOES NOT EXIST, and six of its eight questions are now a button.** The doc shipped 8 read-only queries and said *"paste these into Vida → Engine → SQL"*. **There is no SQL runner.** The only SQL path is `POST /operator/migrations/run`, which executes the reviewed `PENDING_MIGRATIONS` constants and deliberately refuses anything else (correct — not something to widen), and `DATABASE_URL` is mangled so there is no pg connection either. **Eight correct queries with nowhere to run them is a finding that sits there**, and it was mine to fix. **`lib/schema-probe.ts` + `GET /operator/schema-probe` + Vida → Engine → "Schema probe (#558)":** six questions answered through the supabase-js client we already have, **with no SQL at all** — selecting a column that does not exist is an error with a specific code (`42703`/`PGRST204`; `42P01`/`PGRST205` for a table), and selecting one that does is a clean empty result, so **the request IS the probe**. Every call is `select … limit 0` with a head count: nothing written, no row read, no policy in the way. **⚠️ THE RULE THE WHOLE THING TURNS ON: a probe that could not RUN returns ❓ UNKNOWABLE, never "missing".** A bad key, a paused project, a dropped connection and an RLS refusal all arrive as errors, and reading any of them as *"the column is absent"* turns an outage into a confident wrong schema verdict — sending somebody to add a column that was there all along. #565's fourth appearance, and it is the branch that **cannot be tested against a working database**, which is exactly why the classifier is pure. So every client-facing sentence about money was **hand-typed**, which is exactly how this one became a lie the moment the model changed. The three constants now live in **`@kind/shared`** and `onboarding-pack.ts` **re-exports** them, so the API and the client can never disagree about the price; `PACK_SOURCE_TARGET` is derived (`PACK_LEADS * 2`) rather than a second literal. The card is rewritten to what is true and **every figure is derived**. Also caught while in the file: **`packLine` hand-typed `$4`** in its exhausted-pack sentence — now `LEAD_PRICE_USD`. 12 tests (was 5), including a guard that fails if `WALLET_FIRST_PURCHASE_USD` is ever a literal again. Shipping ③ alone would have left a working door behind a gate that refused to open, for precisely the client the fix was for. **Both now read every active ICP and import the EXISTING `splitSourceTarget`** rather than re-deriving it — three copies of a split rule is three chances to drift. `/source` runs sequentially (one shared `try_spend_sourcing` allowance), reports per-ICP failures, 502s only when every run fails, and records `icps_active`/`icps_run` on the audit row. Deliverable: one register (required / optional / dead), `/engine/env` extended to check the required set, and the dead keys deleted along with their code. | 🤝 **30 Jul — DOCUMENTED, AND THE 69 WAS WRONG TWICE OVER.** `docs/ENVIRONMENT.md`: **100 distinct variables** across the three deployed apps — **83** read by the API, **17** by the portal/admin only — each with its tier, **what breaks when it is unset**, and which Railway service holds it. **The first error was method:** a `process.env.X` grep cannot see the **12 variables this repo reaches by indirection** — `process.env[bundle.priceEnvVar]` (`stripe.ts` builds six price-ID names from `PRICING`), `has('NAME')` (`routes/engine.ts`'s probe, the only reader of `PAYSTACK_SECRET_KEY`), and `{ key: 'NAME' }` (`startup-check.ts`'s own table, the only place three `STRIPE_PRICE_LEADGEN_*` names appear at all). The old sweep counted the shape it expected rather than the reads that exist. **The second error was mine, and it is the same defect this repo keeps finding in its own instruments:** my first scanner's comment-stripper was quote-aware but not **regex**-aware, so a literal like `/key:\s*['"]([A-Z]+)['"]/g` desynchronised it and it went blind to comments for the rest of that file — reporting a variable called `FOO` that exists only in a sentence, while a real read could have hidden in the same noise. Caught by red-proving; the count went 96 → **100**. **`startup-check.ts` is the RUNTIME half and covered 22 of 83** — it was reporting on a quarter of the environment and silent about the rest, which at boot reads exactly like an environment with nothing else in it. All 83 now carry a tier. **Two new tiers, and the second one matters:** `platform` (Railway/Node set it — never reported missing, because a boot block that nags about `PORT` is a block nobody reads) and **`parked`, where PRESENCE is the fault, not absence.** The check could previously only complain about a MISSING value, and the most dangerous variable here is dangerous when **set** — `HOUSE_CLIENT_ID` un-parks the Instantly push #593 deliberately parked on 30 Jul. Five are parked: `HOUSE_CLIENT_ID`, `INSTANTLY_API_KEY` (idle since Instantly became warmup-only), and the three dead `PAYSTACK_*`/`FLUTTERWAVE_*` keys that #352 left behind. **Three real faults found by doing this:** ① **`INBOX_SECRET_KEY` was missing from the go-live SENDING capability**, so that block could print ✅ CLIENT SENDING while not one stored mailbox password could be decrypted — **a false green on the one line whose whole job is to prevent false greens**; ② **`SUPABASE_ANON_KEY` was effectively required and listed nowhere** — `middleware/auth.ts` builds a client at module scope and supabase-js throws on an undefined key, so the API does not boot without it; ③ **`DEPLOYMENT_GUIDE.md` held a partial copy that had gone wrong** — ~20 of 100, and it told you to set three `NEXT_PUBLIC_STRIPE_PRICE_LEADGEN_*` variables **nothing in the codebase reads**. All three are now declared in `middleware.ts` **with the reason**: `consent` (a PROSPECT with no account, arriving from the POPIA email — the `?token` is the credential, verified server-side, and gating it would break a legal requirement) · `invite/accept` (**a redirect to /login would DROP the `?token` from the URL, destroying the invite by the act of protecting it** — the page reads the session itself and the API takes the accepting user from the auth token, #266) · `share/[token]` (public IS the feature — an unguessable token so a client's boss can read a report without an account). **The enforcement is the point:** `portal-public-routes.test.ts` walks the real app directory and **fails the gate** if any route is neither gated nor declared, so a new public page cannot be added silently again — red proven by adding one. **It immediately found two routes nobody had named:** `book/[token]` (correct — a prospect books a meeting on a signed 90-day token, #361/#368; now declared) and **`/billing/confirm`, which is the PAYSTACK return URL for a processor deleted in #352** — Stripe returns to `/dashboard/billing?stripe=success` instead, so nothing has routed there for weeks while it sat publicly telling anyone who typed the URL *"Payment received"*. It hid because it lives in the `(dashboard)` route GROUP, so its real URL carries **no `/dashboard` prefix** and the existing gate never covered it. Now redirected. ⚠️ **Finding for the founder, not actioned:** the only UI that hands a client a share link lives in `(dashboard)`, which middleware redirects to `/milla` for every signed-in client — so **no new share link can be generated today, while old tokens still resolve**. Retiring it would break any link a client has already sent, so it is a founder call. `(dashboard)` is redirected and **never deleted** — CORE-MAP: Milla's own billing page is a 13-line wrapper around it. | 🤖 |

Only the five values provable in the enum were candidates; `active` would have booked every unpaid signup as revenue (#340's bug on purpose), `past_due`/`cancelled` are lies about what happened. `paused` is the one whose plain meaning is the founder's sentence. **Both crons retired AND both endpoints now refuse 410** — removing a schedule leaves an endpoint any stale scheduler or run-book can still POST, and a 410 cannot send an email. **Two things found by reading rather than grepping:** ① `/ae/nurture` **never read `trialing` at all** — it read `clients.created_at` inside 14 days, so retiring the status alone would have left it emailing *"Your K.I.N.D trial is live"* to every new signup; ② it was the one lifecycle cron with **no `lifecycleEmailsEnabled()` gate**, so the #480 master kill-switch never covered it. **And one that would have bitten in a fortnight:** the lead-drip's trial-halt required an active sub, an in-period trial, or a past purchase — our own comped house client (#600) has none of those except the trial row, so retiring the trial and leaving that halt would have **silently stopped Client Zero's drip on day 15**. Removed; the wallet check one line above is the real gate. **Grandfathering is explicit, both halves:** `statusGrantsAccess` still honours `trialing` so nothing loses access on deploy, and `20260801_retire_trial_status` converts the rows — **recorded AND registered in `PENDING_MIGRATIONS`** (recording is not running). `/status` reports the remainder as `legacy_trialing` so it is watched to zero, and the counter it replaced was renamed `dormant` because a field called `trialing` reading 0 looks like *"no trials right now"* rather than *"there are no trials"*. **🧪 Your action: Vida → Engine → Run migrations** to clear the legacy rows. **20 tests, red-proven three ways** (trial status restored · cron re-added · a sendable "Subscribe now" put back). | 🤝 |

Verified before 🟢: boxes actually warm + a real send lands in an inbox, which is #553.)* **Cold-email WARMUP — 4 Google mailboxes across 2 domains, warming in Instantly Growth.** `jacques@` + `hello@` on both `kindoutreach.com` and `trykind.org`, each fully authenticated (MX · DKIM 2048 · SPF · DMARC) **before** warmup began — warming a domain that fails authentication wastes the entire three weeks. **Settings, and the one deliberate disagreement with the vendor:** increase/day **1** · reply rate **30%** · weekdays-only **ON** · read-emulation **ON** · slow-warmup left **ON** · **daily cap 25, NOT Instantly's suggested 10.** ⛓️ **CAP DRIFT RECORDED 11 AUG — BOTH NUMBERS KEPT, NEITHER GUESSED.** The founder's own Instantly screen on 11 Aug shows all four ladder boxes reading **`0 of 30`** — i.e. the vendor is showing a daily cap of **30**, not the **25** this row records as what was set up on 4 Aug. **What is founder-verified (11 Aug screen): 30.** **What this row recorded at setup (4 Aug): 25.** **Which was intended is NOT decided here** — it may have been changed deliberately, or the 25 may never have been what was entered; nobody wrote it down at the time, and guessing would be inventing a founder action. ⚠️ **It does not change the ladder's shape** — the clock is still 4 Aug → ~25 Aug and the +1/day climb is unaffected — but it *does* change the number the send ladder should step off from, so **it is worth one founder glance before 25 Aug**. The reasoning below for choosing a cap above the vendor's suggested 10 stands either way. Warmup volume should approximate real send volume: a box warmed to 10/day has built reputation for 10/day, and the jump to ~30 real sends is exactly the spike that gets flagged. At +1/day it still climbs gently. ⚠️ **NO CAMPAIGNS IN INSTANTLY, EVER** — it is a warmup utility only; a campaign there sends outside our engine, outside the approval gate, the opt-out list and the record. ⚠️ **The connection is OAuth, not App Password** — Instantly's own note says app passwords are *"more prone to disconnects"*, and a silent disconnect does not pause warmup, it stops it. Getting there needed a Workspace fix: **Security → API controls → configure Instantly's client ID as a Trusted app**, which is **org-level and therefore covers both domains**. The App Passwords remain the credentials our OWN engine uses (#600) — the two connections are independent. **Clock: started 4 Aug → warm ~25 Aug**, which is the date #553's send ladder counts from. ⛓️ **THE MAILBOX-COUNT QUESTION IS CLOSED — RETIRED 11 Aug by R14** (*"ignore airmail. not relevant"*). **"4 across 2" is correct and is the whole scope:** `jacques@` + `hello@` on `kindoutreach.com` and `trykind.org`, all four founder-verified 11 Aug at **100% health, zero disconnects, 0 sent**. Any other box Instantly displays is out of scope and is never reconciled again — and it is **structurally unsendable-through** in any case, because our path reads `client_inboxes` and requires SMTP credentials (`sending-inbox.ts:63`) that vendor-provisioned boxes do not expose. *(Original 6-Aug text, kept per the chain rule:)* ~~**CORRECTED 6 Aug (#628) — THE COUNT IN THIS ROW MAY BE WRONG.** Instantly's own screen now shows 9 mailboxes across 3 domains, including `nexttrygetkind.com`, which is in no plan on this page and nobody recorded buying. Not reconciled here on purpose; A16 on LAUNCH-PAD owns the confirmation.~~ | 🧍 |

| 366 | 🩷 | **AR-29 · THE REPEAT-BUSINESS KILLER — one ICP only ever sees PAGE ONE of the data, forever.** *(Re-verified in code 26 Jul in the full sweep, and it is worse than the row said.)* `lib/pdl-search.ts:116-117` carries the comment in the code itself: *"PDL deprecated `from`-based pagination — sending it 400s the whole request. **Page 1 only for now**; deeper pages need `scroll_token`."* No `scroll_token` is implemented anywhere. So a client's ICP returns one page, we dedup against every email that client already holds (`icps.ts` anti-dup), and after that **the same ICP produces zero people for that client permanently** — no error, just an empty run. **Why this is a launch blocker and not a nice-to-have:** the cashflow model (#556) says *"the repeat is the business, the pack is the door"* — the $99 pack is **−$52 in month one** and only recovers if they come back for month two. Month two needs **200 more people against the same ICP**, which is exactly what this cannot deliver. It also makes the website's "250M+ contacts" claim false in practice. Fix: implement `scroll_token` paging, and make an exhausted ICP say so out loud instead of returning 0 silently. **FIXED 27 Jul (PR pending):** `scroll_token` paging implemented — the cursor is stored per ICP (`icps.pdl_scroll_token`) with a **fingerprint of the query that produced it**, so an edited ICP discards the token instead of replaying a cursor into an audience the client no longer targets. An exhausted audience now **says so out loud**: a new `audience_exhausted` run outcome carries *"we have reached the end of this audience — widen the ICP"* (never *"no leads matched"*, which blames targeting that was correct to the last person), plus a founder alert on the run that discovers it, and the ICP stops spending grants on a question already answered. Second find while wiring it: the portal's banner was an **allowlist** of two statuses in JSX, so the new outcome rendered *nothing* — inverted in `@kind/shared/run-outcome-banner.ts` so every non-served status shows, including ones nobody has written yet. | 🤖 |

| 199 | 🩷 | *(🔴→🩷 3 Aug — **FOUNDER DID IT**: UptimeRobot is watching `https://api.get-kind.com/health` every 5 min, alerting his email. **Set up as a KEYWORD monitor, not a plain HTTP one, and that distinction is the whole value:** `/health` **always returns 200 by design** so Railway's deploy check passes — whether the database is alive is in the BODY (`"status":"ok"` vs `"degraded"`). A status-code monitor would have sat green through a total database outage, i.e. it would have failed at the one job it was bought for. The monitor alerts when `"status":"ok"` **stops appearing**, which catches both an unreachable API and a live-but-degraded one. 🟢 when he confirms it reading green.)* Production monitoring + alerting (UptimeRobot/BetterStack) | 🤝 |

Now ONE home (`apps/api/src/lib/partner-documents.ts`) serves the pack to both surfaces: **Vida → Partners → Open pack** per seat, and her own vault. **Commission agreement** (moved out of `docs/hiring/`, now a pointer — two copies of a clause means nobody can tell which one was signed) · **NDA** and **IP agreement**, both NEW: the legal pack has required an IP assignment from every contractor since it was written and the document to sign had never existed. ⚠️ **There is no Kevin IP agreement** in this repo or its git history — the founder believed one existed; R5 (6 Aug) locked *"no agreement, no access, no counsel spend"* for Kevin, so it was never drafted. The new one is written from `legal-pack.md` §4/§5.2 instead. · **comp plan** with worked examples · **payout statements**, declared LIVE because they are derived from commission rows and there is no file to store. Every rate and price is **interpolated from the billing constants** — a document saying 8% while the engine pays 5% is a dispute, not a typo — red-proved by typing one rate in and watching the guard fail. Every document carries the *drafted by Claude Code, not by a lawyer, not legal advice* line in its own body, pinned by a test. Also de-typed two money literals in her portal (`PACK_PRICE_USD * 0.2` and a hard-coded "20% land"): rates now travel with the seat from the API. ⛓️ **AMENDED 16 Aug after the founder read the pack — two findings, both fixed before merge.** ① *"not every partner will be in south africa"*: the documents assumed one jurisdiction — SA tax, POPIA by name, payment in **rand**, SA-only lawyer notes. Now **country-neutral**: taxes follow the country of residence, payment is in the partner's local currency (the word *rand* appears in no generated body — a test proves it), privacy names the local law with SA as a worked example, and every review note asks a per-country question. ② *"they give us their address, mobile number etc… i should not need to fill anything out"*: seat creation is now **onboarding** — Vida's card requires address, country and mobile (stored by migration `20260816_partner_contact_details`, the **20th** runner entry, both homes), and the pack interpolates them into the party line, the date and the signature block, so a tailored contract contains **no bracket placeholder at all**. A legacy seat with no details **keeps its brackets** — an invented address reads as agreed, a blank reads as unfinished. All three red-proved by name. ⛓️ **17 Aug — ~~Not built: storing a SIGNED copy per seat~~ IS NOW BUILT** by the onboarding flow (#653): `partner_signed_documents` stores the exact body a person signed, and both vaults serve that snapshot instead of re-generating. The reason it mattered: the pack is generated from the billing constants, so a rate change would otherwise have silently rewritten a signed contract. | 🤖 |

| 273 | 🟡 | **Schema-source consolidation** (carved from #262, 1 Jul) — THREE migration dirs (`supabase/migrations/` · `packages/db/src/migrations/` · `apps/api/src/migrations/`) + the consolidated `supabase/staging-schema.sql` are out of sync (e.g. `webhook_endpoints` lives only in `apps/api/src/migrations/` + is "flagged, not yet migrated"). Consolidate to one source of truth so staging matches prod. Not urgent (staging hygiene). | 🤖 **31 Jul — CONSOLIDATED, and the item's own premise was wrong.** #273 says to make `supabase/migrations/` canonical *"because it is what Vida → Engine runs"*. **It is not, and reading the runner was the first step.** `/operator/migrations/run` calls `runPendingMigrations`, which executes **`PENDING_MIGRATIONS` — a TypeScript constant** — and `pending-migrations.ts` states why: `.sql` files are not copied into `dist/` by tsc, so a file read would work locally and **fail in production**. **No directory has ever been applied to anything**, and **two of the runner's own twelve entries had their file in `apps/api/src/migrations`**, so the claim was not even true of the runner's contents. **The split, measured:** `supabase/migrations` 94 · `apps/api/src/migrations` 19 · `packages/db/src/migrations` 13 — and **not one file was in more than one of them.** So *"where is the migration that created this table?"* depended on which directory you opened first, with nothing to tell you there were two more. **DONE:** all 32 outside files **copied into `supabase/migrations/` with a provenance header and byte-identical bodies** — nothing rewritten, reordered or "fixed" on the way in, because whether a migration was applied to production is a historical fact and editing it while copying destroys the only record of what ran. Originals **kept, not deleted** (rule 3), each carrying a tombstone header naming its canonical copy; **a README in all three directories**. **⚠️ AND ONE MIGRATION HAD NO FILE AT ALL.** `20260726_campaign_copilot_columns` existed **only as a template string inside `pending-migrations.ts`** — a statement the product could apply to production that **nothing in the migration record described**. That is #558 in its purest form, found by mapping all twelve runner keys to disk. Recovered verbatim; the constant remains what runs. **The duplication risk is turned into a guard rather than accepted:** copying leaves two files that can drift, which is the disease #273 is about — so `migration-home.test.ts` asserts every tombstoned body stays **identical** to its canonical copy, that every runner entry **has** a file, that all three READMEs state the distinction that is easiest to get wrong (**recording a migration is not running one**), and that the runner still reads a constant rather than a directory. 13 tests, red-proved five ways. **Stale references updated:** the admin launch checklist pointed nine rows at `packages/db/src/migrations/`; `TECH-STACK.md` described `apps/api/src/migrations/` as **"7 files"** when it held **19** — a doc describing a directory that had almost tripled, the small version of #558. Also `RLS-AUDIT.md`, `SCHEMA-DRIFT.md`, `rls-audit.ts`, `rls-live.ts`, `developer.ts` and `pending-migrations.ts`'s own comments. **⚠️ Consolidating fixes WHERE things live, not WHAT production has** — that stays #558's question, and Vida → Engine → **Schema probe** answers six of it live. | 🤖 |

Note `canceled`→`cancelled` — Stripe spells it with one L, our enum with two. **It also undid the dunning:** `invoice.payment_failed` writes `past_due` and Stripe's paired `subscription.updated` carried `past_due` straight back through the ternary as `active`, within the same second. **#342's trap avoided rather than repeated:** prod's `subscriptions.status` is an ENUM, so writing `incomplete` before the migration fails exactly like `lapsed` does today — and a failed UPDATE leaves the row on its previous value, i.e. still `active`. So every mapping carries a **fallback drawn only from values already in the enum that also deny access**, and the rejection alerts naming the migration. Renewal re-activation widened to the states now reachable (`unpaid`/`incomplete`/`incomplete_expired`), `paused` deliberately excluded — a pause is the client's decision, not a payment failure. | 🤖 |

| 343 | 🟢 | **AR-06 CRITICAL · No cron singleton** — `startCrons()` runs on every API process, no lock/env gate (`index.ts:216`) → 2+ replicas double every email/charge. Gate on `RUN_CRONS` or a pg advisory lock. **FIXED 27 Jul (PR pending):** both halves, because **the env gate alone cannot deliver "never"** — Railway sets variables per SERVICE and every replica inherits them, so `RUN_CRONS=true` reads `true` on *both* replicas and both fire. `RUN_CRONS` is therefore a **kill switch** (unset = ON, deliberately: a missing variable must never silently stop every send, digest and charge), and the guarantee lives in the database: each fire INSERTs into **`cron_claims` keyed (job, slot)** — the first process wins, every other takes a unique violation and stands down. `slot` is the fire time **rounded, not truncated**, to the nearest minute, because two replicas run off two clocks and truncation puts 07:59:59.8 and 08:00:00.2 in different slots — the bug reintroduced by a rounding choice. | 610 | 🩷 | *(**FOUNDER RULED 4 Aug** → **BUILT 4 Aug (Prompt 30):** rotation ships **dormant** — `sendablePool` + `nextFromRotation` spread a batch across every sendable box, least-used first, each box's `daily_cap` respected, all boxes capped = STOP not overflow. Warming still refused. ⚠️ **PARTIAL BY NECESSITY: the counts are per-batch and in memory** — `figsy_sent_emails` has **no column naming the mailbox that sent**, so "sent today by this box" cannot be read from the DB and adding it is a migration the frozen schema forbids. Within a run, exact; across two runs in a day a box could exceed its own cap, still bounded by the global `coldCapReached()`. **The fix is one column, the day migrations return.** Vida shows each box's cap — deliberately NOT a today-count, because we cannot compute one and a fake number is worse than none.)*  *"inbox x 2 yes for now but volume is key"* — `hello@kindoutreach.com` takes the pooled slot as a **documented redefinition** and the send loop learns rotation: **Prompt 30**. Beyond 2: **no workaround** — per-box caps rise 30→50/day as history earns it (~100/day, modelled volume), and boxes 3+4 plug in the day migrations return.)* **[ENGINE] ONE SENDING BOX PER CLIENT IS ALL THE PRODUCT SUPPORTS — the 4-box plan collided with the schema on 4 Aug, found by walking it** *(founder hit `client_inboxes_one_live_per_kind` entering the second of four mailboxes in Vida.)* **Two independent limits, both real:** ① `client_inboxes` allows `kind IN ('pooled','branded')` only, with a unique index on `(client_id, kind) WHERE status IN ('assigned','warming','active')` — so **max two live boxes per client**, and the table's own comment explains why: it models the CLIENT lifecycle (a rented pooled box sending day 1 while their branded box warms, switching ~day 29 with no gap). ② `pickSendingInbox` **ranks the live boxes and takes the top one** — there is **no rotation across mailboxes**, so even two would not both be used. **The 4-box plan assumed a rotation the product does not have.** Consequence today is small: Client Zero sends from `jacques@kindoutreach.com` at a 30/day cap ≈ 600/month, far above the founder's own prospecting need, and the other three keep warming in Instantly as domain-diverse spares. It bites when we want 100–200/day or when a client needs more than one sender. ⚠️ **CANNOT BE FIXED BY MIGRATION — the schema is frozen** (no dashboard, no password). Options when it matters, none free: widen the index and add a `kind` value (needs migrations back), or build rotation in `pickSendingInbox` across the two kinds allowed, or accept one box per client. ⚠️ **DO NOT relabel a branded box as `pooled` to slip past the index** — `pooled` means a rented recycled vendor box that gets released back to a pool that does not exist here, and `pickSendingInbox` ranks branded above pooled. Lying to the schema to defeat a constraint is how the next reader is misled. | 🤖 |
| 615 | 🩷 | **[CLIENT-FACING · #615] VAT EVIDENCE AT ONBOARDING — the default state of every client was "undocumented", which is the state that costs 20%** *(founder-ruled 4 Aug: "as part of onboarding we capture their company information in documents section… I would say all clients.")* **⚠️ WHY IT IS A TAX QUESTION, NOT A FORM QUESTION.** We are a UK Ltd. For a **business** customer the place of supply is where the CUSTOMER belongs — so an overseas business is outside UK VAT and an EU business self-accounts under the reverse charge. **But the evidence rule is unforgiving: you must hold proof they are a business.** No proof means consumer treatment, which means **20% on top**. Today `company_registration` and `vat_number` exist on `clients` as **optional fields on a Settings page nobody is required to visit** — so every client defaults to undocumented. **BUILT:** `packages/shared/src/company-details.ts` (pure) + `POST /clients/company-details` + a **Documents** card explaining *why* we ask (a client who is not told skips the form; one who is told it keeps 20% off their invoice fills it in). **⚠️ THE SOLE-TRADER PROBLEM, and why a tick rather than a blank:** a legitimate client may genuinely have no VAT number. Forcing a value makes them **invent one**, and an invented tax ID is **worse than a blank** — it looks like evidence, so nobody chases it, and it is the thing an auditor reads. So "not registered" is an **explicit recorded answer**: blank means *nobody asked*, the sentinel means *we asked and this is the answer*. Different facts, different invoice treatment, therefore different values. Ticking the box AND supplying a number is refused (both cannot be true); typing the sentinel by hand is refused (that would forge the answer). **A SENTINEL, NOT A COLUMN** — the schema is frozen, so `NOT_REGISTERED` is a reserved value in the existing `vat_number`, the same shape as `PERIOD_END_SENTINEL`; a real boolean lands the day migrations return. **⚠️ IT GATES ONBOARDING COMPLETION AND NOTHING ELSE, enforced by test:** an approval, a payment or a send must NEVER consult it — every client who signed up before this existed has empty fields, and locking them out of the product they pay for over a form would be a far worse bug than the one it fixes. Where enforcement goes beyond onboarding is **a founder decision, deliberately not taken in code**. The general settings PATCH keeps both fields optional for the same reason. **D4 (the Vida "no tax ID" badge) is PENDING THIS PR'S MERGE** — `vatBadge` lives in this branch's shared package, and duplicating a shared module across two branches is the drift this repo keeps paying for. ⚠️ **CLIENT-FACING → the founder previews before it goes live (RULEBOOK §11); 🟡 until he approves, then 🟣.** Red-proved 3 ways. 17 tests. | 🤖 | **⚠️ COMPLETED 5 Aug (C6):** `vatBadge` shipped here and **Vida never rendered it**, so "no tax ID on file" was a fact the operator could only find by opening one client at a time — on the exact list where they decide who to chase, and VAT evidence is the difference between charging 20% and eating it. The clients list now carries the badge in all three tones (amber missing · grey declared-not-registered via the `NOT_REGISTERED` sentinel · green on file), rendered from the **SHARED** function with **no local VAT rule and no house/demo special case** — a second opinion about VAT status is how the client page and the operator board start disagreeing about who owes 20%. `vat_number` rides the worklist query that was already being made: no new round trips.

So the panel was a guaranteed **401 on every load**: an audit nobody could run. Two faults in one, since a direct call that ever did authenticate would also be a hole in the operator audit trail. **The build was green throughout** — the component compiled, the page built, the gate passed, and it only breaks in a browser nobody had opened. `admin-proxy-only.test.ts` now sweeps every admin component and fails any `/operator/` fetch not going through `/api/proxy/`, red-proved against the real pre-fix file.)* **[VIDA] THE HOUSE-ACCOUNT AUDIT — an instrument, because there is no console to look in** *(4 Aug. Both right; together they leave the house account inside a rule whose stated purpose is *"a bill we pay to keep an inbox warm for **nobody**"* — **we are the nobody**. Nil impact today (it pauses campaigns `WHERE status='active'`, there are none, and it returns silently having paused zero). **From ~25 Aug it is live:** a campaign going active while the last approval is >30 days old gets paused at the next 08:40 run, with a churn-risk alert about ourselves. An approval resets the clock, so the normal order (approve → run) avoids it — a trap for the abnormal order, on send-day. **Founder ruling owed: exempt the house account, or clear the clock before send-day.** — **PHASE B BUILT 4 AUG (Prompt 31), on the founder's rulings.** Three panel fixes + two writes. ① The panel **no longer runs itself** — it shipped with `useEffect(() => { void run() }, [run])`, so opening Engine filled four screens with findings and the founder's reply was *"i didnt run anything"*; the button is now the only trigger. ② The **`can send` chip was status-blind** — `has_smtp ? 'can send' : 'no SMTP details'` — so a **warming** box with credentials saved rendered a **green "can send"** while `pickSendingInbox` refuses it outright (*sending on a warming mailbox is what un-warms it*). That is the state of **all four of the founder's boxes right now**: the board and the sender said opposite things and nothing was red. It now asks the send path itself (`boxSendVerdict`). ③ The **warm-up fraction was hardcoded `/14`** and the route clamped the day to 14, while these Google boxes warm for **21** (`DEFAULT_WARMUP_DAYS`) — so around **18 Aug it would have read "14/14 · ready" a full week early**, on the one screen used to decide when sending starts. The length is now DERIVED from the row's own `warmup_ready_at − warmup_started_at`, and with no ready date **no fraction is shown at all** rather than a guessed denominator. ④ **Zero the house wallet** — gated `POST /operator/house-audit/zero-wallet`: house-only via `decideHouseClient` (never by name), typed phrase `ZERO THE HOUSE WALLET`, refuses an already-zero balance, writes `operator_audit_log`, and **inserts NO ledger row** — no money ever moved, so recording a movement would put a fabricated event in the one table that IS the audit trail. Then **$4,000 hunting budget** through the existing `manual_grant` comp mechanism (a constant, not a request field — an endpoint taking an amount is a "give any client any money" surface). ⑤ **`POST /operator/seed-data/wipe-client`** — ⚠️ **the first delete of a NON-DEMO client row in the product.** `purgeDemoClient` refuses anything not `is_demo` and its own comment calls that *"the whole safety of this function"*; Stripe Test and ACME are not demo rows, so a new guard had to be at least as strong. Four of them: `seed-wipe.ts`'s `classify` **imported, never re-implemented** (real money outranks every label) · an explicit **id** refusal for the house account that survives the classifier being fooled · an id refusal for every `is_demo` row · the client's own **company name, typed**. Plus a structural fifth: **the house account must RESOLVE before anything is deleted** — if we cannot say which row is Client Zero we are not in a position to delete another one. `wipeMbf` is imported for the table list rather than re-listed, so it cannot fall behind. Red-proved: the confirm-phrase gate · the house refusal fired AT the house id · the chip on a warming box · the denominator on day 14 of 21. | 🤝 |
| 612 | 🩷 | **[ENGINE · FOUNDER-RULED 4 Aug] THE SEQUENCE QUALITY GATE — world-class copy, enforced before a warm box ever sends** *(founder: "our sequence and outreach must be world class. shit emails out = zero meetings booked. for all clients.")* **⚠️ WHY IT HAD TO EXIST BEFORE 25 AUG AND NOT AFTER.** Four Google boxes finish warming ~25 Aug and **the FIRST sends out of them set those domains' reputation permanently** — a bad first batch does not merely book zero meetings, it burns three weeks of warming and the domains with it. funnel lands nearer 500:1 or 1,000:1 is decided in the words, not the sending. **`lib/sequence-quality.ts` — PURE**, so every rule is provable without emailing anyone, which is the only way to test a linter whose failure mode is *“a real prospect received this.”* **TWO SEVERITIES, and the split is the design:** **HARD blocks activation** — reserved for things that are WRONG, not merely weak (no opt-out anywhere · spam-trigger vocabulary · a link or attachment in step 1 · no personalisation in step 1's opening lines · a bare “just bumping” follow-up · >7 steps · two steps landing the same day). **WARN never blocks** — subject length, 50–125-word body, one-ask-per-email, Flesch–Kincaid grade >8, tired openers. *A warn that blocked would teach operators to route around the gate, and a gate people route around protects nothing.* **⚠️ ONE HELPER, THREE ACTIVATION ROUTES.** A campaign goes live from **three** places (`/campaign/save` creating or patching to active · `/campaign/:id/status` · `/campaign/start`) and **a gate wired into two of them is not a gate, it is a detour sign** — all three call `sequenceGateFor`, and a fourth route added later fails the wiring test rather than silently opening a hole. **Pausing is deliberately never gated:** stopping bad copy must stay one click away. **SAVE IS NOT BLOCKED, ACTIVATION IS** — work in progress must be savable; what must not happen is putting it in front of a stranger. **FIGSY's own draft goes through the same gate before it is returned**, because a draft arriving “from the AI” carries an authority a hand-typed one does not — exactly when a bad email gets waved past. **The preview lints the RAW template, never the token-filled render** — linting `rendered` would report every correct sequence as un-personalised (tokens already replaced), and *a rule that cries wolf on correct copy is a rule the operator learns to ignore.* **Fails OPEN on a read error, deliberately:** if the sequence table cannot be READ we do not know the copy is bad, and refusing to start a campaign because the database hiccuped would make an outage look like a copy problem (#565's shape). **✅ A CLEAN PASS NAMES WHAT IT CHECKED** rather than rendering as silence — the #565/#576/#581/#611 lesson: a screen that shows nothing when all is well is indistinguishable from a check that never ran. **⚠️ TWO OF MY OWN GUARDS WERE WRONG AND RED-PROVING IS THE ONLY REASON THEY ARE RIGHT.** ① The wiring test counted `sequenceGateFor(` — which **also matches the function's own definition** — so `>= 3` was satisfied by *1 definition + 2 calls*: **deleting a gate from a live route left the test GREEN.** Now counts `await sequenceGateFor(` and asserts exactly 3. ② The opt-out rule accepted `won't bother you again` but **rejected `will not bother you again`** — a guard that recognises only one spelling of a promise teaches people to write the other. **And it broke a neighbour's test honestly:** `vida-honesty.test.ts` sliced `+ 1400` characters from `/campaign/start` and went red when the gate pushed `writeOperatorAudit` past that window — **the property held, the WINDOW moved.** Rewritten to slice to the next route registration, then red-proved by flipping the audit action. **NO migrations** (everything computed at request time) · nothing enables sending · warmup/ladder/`AUTO_OUTREACH_ENABLED` untouched. 50 tests, red-proved four ways. — **⚠️ PART B, 4 Aug: THE GATE COVERED EVERY PATH EXCEPT THE ONE THAT SENDS.** Found by Fable's verify of the OPEN PR, **not by anything going red** — a green gate said nothing about the enrol path. **GAP ①:** the words that actually send live on the **enrollment**, not the template. `routes/figsy.ts` enrols with `appliedSequence ? buildDraftFromSequence(...) : await generateSequence(...)` — and with **no applied sequence the AI writes fresh copy per lead that nothing looked at.** Part A gated templates and the drafts an operator *reads*; this is the copy the send loop actually emails. Now gated at **both** enrol sites, **before `chargeFigsyEnroll`** — #332's *charge-first* means the charge gates the ENROL, it was never a reason to take a credit for a lead we are about to refuse. One regenerate, then **skip with the rule NAMED** in `skip_reasons` (a bare *"3 skipped"* sends the reader hunting in the wrong place). **Demo exempt** — #453 leaves `next_send_at` null so a demo can never reach a human, and #329 already settled that breaking the demo removes the sales tool. **GAP ②:** `sequenceGateFor` linted the client's **newest saved** row while a campaign carries an **applied** one (`settings.sequence`, written by `figsy.ts`) — save a clean draft over an older bad applied one and the gate green-lit copy that was never going to send. Judged by the template rules, **every draft the product writes hard-fails on `no_personalisation` AND `link_in_step_1` — 100% of leads skipped, reported as a copy problem.** So the linter gained a **`rendered` mode** that changes exactly three things: personalisation is proved by the person's own details appearing in the opening, the client's **own** booking URL is not counted as a generic link (**but is surfaced as a WARN** — never silently forgiven), and nothing else moves. **🧍 FOUNDER RULING OWED — a real product conflict, not a lint detail: #612 says no link in the first touch and `generateSequence` is told to put one there.
