| 613 | 🩷 | **[MONEY · #613] REVENUE RECORDED HONESTLY — what Stripe SETTLED, not the price list** *(4 Aug, from the founder's "full audit of all expenses" sweep.)* **⚠️ EVERY money row in this product recorded the price we QUOTED.** `routes/stripe.ts` wrote `amount` from `session.metadata.amountUsd` — the figure we put INTO the checkout — and the subscription path wrote `STRIPE_SUBSCRIPTIONS[product].priceUsd`, a hardcoded constant. **Nothing anywhere read `balance_transaction`.** So a discount, a partial refund, a dispute or a currency conversion was **invisible**, and **no figure in the console reconciled to the bank**. On a $299 sold in USD into a GBP account the gap is real money: card fee 1.5–3.25% PLUS Stripe's FX cut, so **roughly £210–£213 arrives against a console saying $299**. **BUILT:** `lib/stripe-settlement.ts` (pure) + `readSettlement`/`annotateSettlement`/`listRecentSettlements` + read-only `GET /operator/revenue/reconcile` + a Vida panel on Billing, *"What the bank actually received"*. **⚠️ ANNOTATION, NOT COLUMNS — and it is recorded as the lesser fix it is.** The honest schema is `stripe_fee_usd`/`net_usd`/`settlement_currency`; **the schema is frozen**, so the facts are appended to each row's existing `note` text as `[settled: gross … · stripe fee … · net … · payout currency GBP · fx …]`. Readable and greppable; **NOT summable** — no dashboard can total them. The format is pinned by test precisely because a future backfill will parse exactly that string. **BEST-EFFORT BY CONSTRUCTION:** the client's money is credited FIRST and never depends on this — a Stripe outage costs an annotation, never a payment. **The verdict language matters:** `fee_only` is the EXPECTED healthy state (the ledger records the price, the bank receives price-minus-fee) and rendering that ordinary gap as red on every row would train the reader to ignore the column; a real `mismatch` is when the GROSS differs. A failed read is **`unknown`, never `match`** (#565/#576's rule). **⚠️ IT CANNOT MAKE OLD ROWS HONEST** — annotation begins with payments from 4 Aug forward; historical rows keep their list price. **Red-proved 3 ways**, and the ordering proof caught **my own test being wrong**: it compared each stamp against the FIRST ledger insert anywhere in the file, so moving a stamp above its own insert still passed. Re-scoped per payment path. 19 tests. | 🤖 |

Nothing may be re-tagged `verified` without somebody opening the real page. **Also corrected: Stripe 3.5% → 5% all-in**, because we charge USD and bank GBP and the old figure counted the card fee while silently ignoring the conversion. The cost of failing open is that OUR campaign might be paused — visible, alerted, one click to undo. The cost of failing closed is that **everyone** is silently exempt and a rule that saves real money quietly stops working. **Demo exemption unchanged; a real paying client on the identical clock is still suspended** — asserted by test, because the feature's whole value is that it still fires for the people it was written for. Red-proved 3 ways (remove the house branch · unwire the cron · invert it to fail closed). 14 tests. | 🤖 |

| 617 | 🩷 | **[LEGAL · #617] THE PECR PASS — we must not cold-email a UK sole trader** *(5 Aug, the only pre-send build not started).* UK PECR reg. 22 bans unsolicited marketing email to **individual subscribers**; Ltd/PLC/LLP are exempt, and that exemption is the entire legal basis for B2B cold email. **A UK sole trader is an individual subscriber** — same protection as a consumer. Without it the client would have been charged for a lead we can never legally email. **Plus a send-time net** at the same chokepoint as the opt-out check, catching rows enrolled before this shipped; suppressed rows are stood down (`next_send_at: null`) so the cron stops re-picking them. Missing country **sends but is NAMED** — suppressing every unknown would delete most of the book on an enrichment gap. Non-UK out of scope. Demo unaffected. **21 tests, red-proved 7 ways.** ⚠️ Not legal advice — a conservative default the founder can loosen. | 🤖 |

Checkboxes, one action per line, no wall-clock promises. Covers the warmup week · pre-flight (test send · **mail-tester ≥9/10 or DO NOT LAUNCH** · cap chip must not read *No send cap set* · baseline screenshot) · the flip · the first-hour watch **including #620's skip line** · **three tripwires each paired with its kill action** · the kill order (deferred is not deleted) · and **the money walk if a client pays** (per #562 the pack must NOT credit the wallet; per #619 `Comped` belongs only to the house account). | 🤖 | **⚠️ CORRECTED 5 Aug, same session — THE REPLY PATH WAS THE GAP THE RUNBOOK MISSED.** Checking whether the founder needed to watch the outreach mailbox at all, I read the send chain end to end: outreach is **sent** by SMTP from the warmed Google mailbox (`sendAs` → nodemailer), but every email carries a **`Reply-To:`** from `FIGSY_COLD_REPLY_TO` (→ `FIGSY_REPLY_TO` → default `hello@get-kind.com`) — so replies do **not** come back to the sending mailbox. And ingestion is **only** Resend's inbound webhook (`/figsy/replies/inbound`, fails closed without `RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET`); **there is no IMAP poller on the Google boxes.** If that reply-to address is unwatched or its inbound is not routed to Resend, **every reply is lost and the Unibox looks exactly like "nobody replied"** — a campaign with no return path, discovered on send-day. Four `CHECK:` items added, including the page's single most important pre-flight test: **send a test, reply to it from another address, confirm it lands in the Unibox.**
| 622 | 🩷 | **[ENGINE · #622] SEND CAPACITY — and the read-first pass changed the build** *(5 Aug, under A6's standing ruling.)* D1 read *"boxes 3+4 into the engine"*, which sounds like a code change. **It is not: there is NO two-box limit anywhere in the engine.** `sendablePool` returns **every** box that is sendable and credentialled and `nextFromRotation` spreads across all of them least-used-first — four boxes have always been supported. Boxes 3+4 are excluded by exactly one thing: their **status is `warming`**, and warming boxes are refused **correctly** (sending on a warming mailbox is what un-warms it). **So D1 is a STATUS FLIP on the mailbox rows when warmup completes — a founder action, recorded in the runbook, not a build.** Likewise A6's ladder was already enforced and already topped out at exactly 50/day — but the steps lived as **bare literals**, so the ladder the founder rules on and the ladder the engine enforces matched by coincidence. **BUILT:** `WARMUP_LADDER` + `WARMUP_STEADY_CAP` named in one place, `warmupRampCap` reads off them; behaviour byte-identical (the pre-existing ramp tests pass untouched). **Nothing flipped on:** `AUTO_OUTREACH_ENABLED` stays off and the warming guard is unchanged. **13 tests, red-proved 3 ways** (soften the warming guard · drop the top rung to 30 · cap the pool at 2 boxes — the limit D1 assumed existed). | 🤖 |
| 623 | 🩷 | **[MONEY · #623] THE "$ IN" FIGURE WAS CALCULATED, NOT COUNTED — and the founder's own money walk found it** *(5 Aug, A9 Walk 1).* He approved two leads on Client Zero and asked why the board did not show **$8 more earned**. It did not — and it was **right** not to: both contacts were already paid for under **#424 charge-once**, so no money moved. The wallet, the pack counter and the charge were all honest. **The board was not.** Vida's *"$ in"* was `PACK_PRICE_USD + (approved − 100) × LEAD_PRICE_USD` — **arithmetic on the approval COUNT** — so on any client with a repeat contact it prints cash that **never arrived**. **This is #619's failure pointing the other way:** #619 was a comped account claiming a payment; this is a real client's board **inflating what they paid us**. Same law, opposite direction — a money surface must never round up. **BUILT:** pure `moneyInUsd()` in `onboarding-pack.ts`, beside `fundedVia()` so the two read the same rows and cannot drift. **Counted off the ledger:** only rows that EVIDENCE money moving — a purchase type carrying a provider reference, the same test the wipe guard and `fundedVia` already apply. Deliberately excluded, each for its own reason: `manual_grant` (a comp — entitles work, no cash) · `usage $0` (pack-covered, already paid for by the $299) · `wallet_charge` (the client spending money they already loaded — counting it books the same dollar twice) · unreferenced purchase rows (hand-made, not evidenced). **⚠️ AND IT FOUND THE NEXT OVERSTATEMENT DOWN THE ROAD, which the brief did not ask for:** refunds are written as `type: 'refund'` with a **negative** amount, **outside** `PURCHASE_TX_TYPES` — and the worklist query never even fetched them. Summing purchases alone would leave a **fully refunded client still reading "$299 in"**. So a referenced refund **subtracts**, and the query widened to `CASH_TX_TYPES`. *"$ in"* now means **net cash we actually kept**. **⚠️ NOT CLAMPED AT ZERO:** an over-refund reads negative, because that is a real anomaly and `Math.max(0, …)` would be this same bug a third time, in the direction of looking tidy. **The engine was never touched** — wallet, pack counter, charge path, `fundedVia` all unchanged; this is display honesty only. `hasFunded` explicitly re-scoped so the newly-fetched refund rows cannot read as funding and silently change what `client-step` gates on. **18 tests, red-proved 6 ways** (ignore refunds · count unreferenced rows · clamp the anomaly · put the count arithmetic back · narrow the query · let refunds count as funding). | 🤖 |
| 624 | 🩷 | **[LAUNCH · #624] THE REPLY-PATH ROW ANSWERED HALF THE QUESTION — and the half it skipped is the one that loses every reply** *(5 Aug; closes the LAST 🤖 item on the critical list).* The System check already had a *Reply path* row: it verified `RESEND_WEBHOOK_SECRET` and counted replies, **and never asked where replies are addressed.** Outreach is SENT by SMTP from the warmed Google mailbox, but every email carries a `Reply-To` from `COLD_REPLY_TO` — `FIGSY_COLD_REPLY_TO` → `FIGSY_REPLY_TO` → **a silent hardcoded `hello@get-kind.com`** — and replies arrive ONLY through Resend's inbound webhook. **So the screen could report green while every reply went to a mailbox whose inbound was never wired to Resend:** a campaign with no return path, and the failure is completely silent — an empty Unibox reads as *"nobody answered"*, never as *"we lost them all"*. Found chasing the founder's Gmail question, three weeks before send-day, on the one path he executes ALONE. **BUILT:** pure `replyPathVerdict()` in `reply-path.ts` (provable without a network, same pattern as `coldView`/`pecrVerdict`); the probe gathers facts, the function judges. So a listed domain is **necessary, not sufficient**, and the row says so in words and still sends the founder to the live-fire test. An unreachable Resend is **NOT-MEASURED, never OK** — *"configured"* without proof is the exact false green this build ends. **And the same zero means opposite things either side of send-day:** 0 replies before the first send is expected and said so; 0 replies *after* mail has gone out is called out as **the signature of a dead return path**. | 632 | 🩷 | **[MARKETING · founder-ordered 11 Aug] THE MARKETING PLAN — how K.I.N.D gets known, and the recurring actions that make it a system rather than a document.** Reserved by **R6 (6 Aug)** for exactly this and minted now, not re-numbered. Lives in `docs/marketing/` as **operational** docs (like `SEND-DAY-RUNBOOK.md`), **not a fifth core doc** — the four-doc contract is untouched: no status here, no launch execution here. Six files plus a README: `MARKETING-PLAN.md` (the master — strategy, the growth loop, and the **daily/weekly/monthly** action tables) · `founder-led-marketing-system.md` · `beehiiv-setup-checklist.md` · `founder-content-playbook.md` · `paid-ads-phase-plan.md` · `marketing-metrics-and-iteration.md`. **Built from the founder's own 36-page M&V Lead Generation Master Playbook** (Core Four · More-Better-New · Rule of 100 · the one-page plan · the scripts) — an M&V adaptation of publicly described Alex Hormozi / Acquisition.com concepts, not affiliated with or a reproduction of $100M Leads. ⚠️ **TWO OF THE PLAYBOOK'S SECTIONS ARE SUPERSEDED BY LATER FOUNDER RULINGS and are marked as such rather than rewritten:** its **section 02 Brand Architecture** (*"M&V is the brand"*) is dead under **R9** (6 Aug, *"kill it"*) — public copy says **K.I.N.D**; and its **section 06 The Offer** (*"Locked: one free allocation of 10 researched prospects"*, *"make it the exact offer everywhere"*) is narrowed by **R1** (6 Aug) to a SALES TOOL — *"never pre-built, never in code, never on the site"*. The playbook is not wrong; it was written before the rulings. ⚠️ **THE PUBLIC HALF IS GATED AND SHIPPED SWITCHED OFF.** **R2** (6 Aug, **re-affirmed 11 Aug** — see R20) holds: LinkedIn **company** page yes, personal announcement no. So founder posting and all paid ads are built ready and marked as gated; **~80% of the system runs with both locks fully in force** — warm outreach, the list, the welcome sequence, the content bank. Paid ads additionally gated on **R7** (no real money). ⚠️ **TWO HARD TECHNICAL RULES, stated in three of the files because they are the expensive ones:** never authenticate `kindoutreach.com` or `trykind.org` in beehiiv (those are the four mailboxes warming since 4 Aug — one blast burns them and resets send-day), and never change `gettingkind.com` MX (Resend inbound reply-capture, proved in A18; it breaks **silently**). Use `news.get-kind.com` or beehiiv's own domain. ⚠️ **ONE UNVERIFIED NUMBER, flagged in two files rather than papered over:** beehiiv's pricing was **not read** — `beehiiv.com` is blocked by the environment's egress proxy — so the zero-added-cost claim rests on an assumed free entry tier the founder must confirm. **The docs are wired into `doc-lint`'s DOCS list in the same PR**, because the one thing 6 Aug proved about an unlinted doc is that it rots: `sales-playbook.md` carried a "PRICING (locked)" header quoting the retired ladder precisely because nothing checked it. | 🧍 |
| 644 | 🟢 | **[A11 WALK · CLIENT-FACING · FOUND 12 Aug] A CLIENT CANNOT REACH THEIR OWN REPLIES FROM MILLA — the rail names the reply and nothing opens it.** Founder, walking A11 J5: *"its there but not allowing me to press"*. **Verified in code, not inferred:** `components/milla/MillaShell.tsx:176` renders each recent reply as a plain `<div>` — **no `<Link>`, no `href`, no `onClick`, no router push.** It is display text by construction. And the Milla rail has no Replies or Inbox entry at all (New leads · Pipeline · Meetings · My campaign · My ICP · Documents · Reports · Coaching), **so there is no other route either** — while the reply screen itself exists and works (`(dashboard)/dashboard/inbox/page.tsx`, with a working AI-draft handler that resets state on success, fallback AND error). **The feature is built; nothing navigates to it.** ⚠️ **This is the #626 shape — a screen naming an action nobody can perform — on the single thing the client is paying for.** The whole promise is *"we run your outbound, you approve the leads worth pursuing"*, and the reply is the outcome of that. A client with a hot reply sees it exists and cannot open it. **Found by pressing it. 2,157 tests did not, because nothing asserts that a rendered reply is reachable.** | 🤖 ✅ **FIXED AND WALKED 🟢 12 Aug — founder: *"all works"*.** He clicked a reply in Milla's rail and it opened. `/milla/replies` now exists (rendering the REAL inbox, not a second copy that would drift), the rail carries a **Replies** entry badged with the live count, and the recent-reply rows are `<Link>`s instead of inert `<div>`s. **Guarded by `replies-reachable.test.ts`, which asserts REACHABILITY rather than rendering** — 4 of its 6 assertions fail against the pre-fix shell. ⚠️ **The lesson worth keeping:** 2,157 tests missed this because every one asked whether something *renders*. None asked whether what renders can be *reached*. "Visible but unreachable" is indistinguishable from absent to the person paying for it. |
| 645 | 🩷 | **[A11 WALK · FOUND 12 Aug] #66's INVENTORY ROW SENDS YOU TO THE WRONG APP — "Unibox 'Help me reply'" does not exist in Vida.** The row reads *"R7 Unibox 'Help me reply' (Claude draft)"*, so a walker opens **Vida → Unibox** and hunts. `grep -rli "help me reply"` returns **four files, every one of them in `apps/portal` (the CLIENT app)** — `(dashboard)/dashboard/inbox`, `(dashboard)/dashboard/figsy/replies`, `(v2)/v2/inbox`, `(v2)/v2/gallery` — and **zero in `apps/admin`.** The Vida Unibox (224 lines) does have its own reply capability, which is what makes the row's wording plausible enough to waste time on. ⚠️ **Cost of this class of error is the walk itself:** A11 has 281 items and a row that names the wrong screen burns founder minutes and produces a false "can't find". **Fix is the row, not the code.** Blocked behind **#644** in any case — the client screen it actually lives on is currently unreachable. | 🤖 ✅ **FIXED 12 Aug** — #66's row now names the client portal and cites the grep. Row-level fix; no code involved. |
| 646 | 🩷 | **[OPS · FOUND + FIXED 12 Aug on the A11 walk] THE COCKPIT SHOWED A RAND EXCHANGE RATE TO A UK FOUNDER.** The operator cockpit's header read **`Live stats · R16.19/$`** — rand-per-dollar, on the daily dashboard of a **UK Ltd that charges in USD and banks in GBP**. A leftover from the project's South-African origin. **The fix was one line of arithmetic that was already being fetched:** `open.er-api.com/v6/latest/USD` returns every rate in one response, so `rates.GBP` was sitting in the same JSON the rand was read from and was simply ignored — `fxLabel` now shows **£/$**, with a GBP fallback so it can never fall back to a rand. ⚠️ **THE ZAR MACHINERY IS DELIBERATELY UNTOUCHED.** `amount_zar` is a real column holding real historic rows and `zarToUsd` converts them; rewriting revenue maths that has not been re-verified would trade a cosmetic defect for a money one. What changed is the number a human reads. ⚠️ **Why a wrong currency label is worth an item at all:** it is not dangerous on its own — it is dangerous because it teaches you to stop trusting your own dashboard, and the cockpit is where the cost floor and the burn are reported. | 🤖 |
| 647 | 🔴 | **[OPS · FOUND 12 Aug, deliberately NOT fixed the same day] THE REVENUE PAGE STILL HEADLINES "MRR (ZAR)".** `admin/src/app/revenue/page.tsx:261` renders **`MRR (ZAR)`** as `R${stats.mrrZar}` and converts to USD through `zarToUsd`. Same root as **#646** — the South-African origin — but **this one is money maths, not a label**, so it was left alone rather than rewritten in a session that had not verified where `mrrZar` comes from or what depends on it. **Recording it beats a quiet half-fix:** the founder banks GBP, charges USD, and his revenue screen reports rand. ⚠️ **Do not "just change the currency"** — establish first whether `amount_zar` rows are legacy-only or still written, because the answer decides whether this is a display fix or a data migration. | 🤖 |
| 648 | 🩷 | **[OPS · FOUND + FIXED 12 Aug on the A11 walk] TWO OPERATOR SCREENS SAID "AWAITING" ABOUT COMPLETELY DIFFERENT QUEUES.** The System page reported *"5 awaiting a decision"* for a client while the Lead queue said *"Nothing awaiting approval"* — and **both were correct**. `system-probes.ts` counts **LEADS the CLIENT has not approved** (`leads.revealed_at is null`); the Lead queue calls `listPendingDrafts()`, which counts **EMAIL DRAFTS awaiting the OPERATOR**. **No data was ever wrong; the words collided.** During the first A11 walk the founder read one screen, opened the other, found it empty, and a step was spent establishing that neither was lying. **Renamed for WHO decides:** *"awaiting the CLIENT's approval"* and *"No drafts awaiting YOUR approval"*, and the queue's subtitle now says outright that it is not the client-approval queue. ⚠️ **This is the cheapest class of defect to fix and among the most expensive to leave** — it costs a few minutes every single time, forever, and it reads as a bug in the data rather than in the wording. | 🤖 |

| 653 | 🩷 | **[R42, founder-ruled 16 Aug] THE PARTNER ONBOARDING FLOW — invite → she completes her pack → she signs → he counter-signs → live.** *"i create the partner seat. it should send them an email notifying them. they then sign up and complete their information pack. this renders all docs. they sign. i recieve docs i sign and they then go live on partner. once they have this they are notified."* **What was wrong:** a seat was created SILENTLY (no email ever sent — she could only get in via a forgot-password nobody had told her about), the OPERATOR typed her address for her, nothing was ever signed, and the seat was born **live with a working referral code before anyone had opened a document**. **Built:** migration `20260816_partner_onboarding_flow` (21st runner entry, both homes) adds `onboarding_state` + invite token + `payout_details` + the `partner_signed_documents` table · three real emails (invite → *awaiting your signature* → *you are live*), each returning a boolean the caller records, because a silently failed email leaves a person waiting for ever · her own `/partner-onboarding` page: set password, her details, **how she gets paid** (the agreement promises payment against an invoice and nothing collected the bank details), then read + click-sign each document (typed name + tick) · Vida shows the state, an *awaiting you* banner, the counter-sign box and **Archive** (never delete — commission rows must outlive a seat). **THE MONEY GATE:** `/partners/ref/:code` now resolves ONLY for a live seat, so an unsigned partner cannot land a client. **THE FROZEN COPY:** what was signed is stored verbatim and served instead of a regeneration — otherwise a rate change would rewrite a signed contract. **THE GRANDFATHER:** `onboarding_state` defaults to `'active'`, so Demmy Oshodi and KIND-JACQUES (live today, no signatures on file) are untouched, and an unrun migration cannot switch a working code off either. | 663 | 🩷 | **🌍 THE LAUNCH-COUNTRY ALLOWLIST — R45 stops being a sentence and becomes six gates (20 Aug).** R45 ruled on 19 Aug that the first send is US + UK, and its enforcement column said *"nothing yet"* for a day: the ruling existed and no code asked the question. Built: `LAUNCH_SEND_COUNTRIES` in `@kind/shared` with tolerant matching (`us`/`USA`/`United States`, `uk`/`GB`/`Scotland`…) in the style of `pecr.ts`'s `UK_COUNTRIES`, and six gates that hold anything else — **held, never deleted:** the lead keeps its row and sends the day he opens its country. ⚠️ **The gate the prompt did not ask for is the one that made "never charged" true.** The founder's ruling was *"never charged"*, and reading the order of events showed the enrol gates cannot deliver it: `try_charge_wallet` fires at `approve-lead.ts` step 4 and `autoEnrollLead` — where the enrol gate lives — is called at the BOTTOM of the same function. A held lead would have been revealed, charged $4, then refused: the charge-then-refuse #332 forbids, and the same shape as the #625 no-campaign bug. So a sixth gate sits at **step 3d, before the till**, un-claiming the lead so it returns to the queue intact. ⚠️ **And a second one nobody asked for, because the allowlist otherwise costs money to enforce:** `pdl-search.ts` pushes an ICP's `geographies` straight into the PDL query as `location_country`, and `SUPPORTED_COUNTRIES` — the list the ICP builder SUGGESTS — is entirely African. A client picking Nigeria meant PDL budget spent sourcing leads every send gate would then hold, with no error anywhere. The fence now refuses a held country at the **target-list save**, on the schema rather than in the three handlers, so the PATCH door's `.partial()` inherits it and a fourth door added later does too (the min-20 lesson). ⚠️ **It INVERTS PECR on a blank country, deliberately:** `pecrVerdict` allows unknown (refusing on a missing field would delete most of the book on a legal test that may not even apply); this holds it, because we cannot claim a lead is in the US or the UK when nothing on the row says so. **That is not free, and the cost is now COUNTED rather than estimated (20 Aug, parsed live from the founder's own `leads` export):** 206 rows — **166 real leads with no country · 40 `.invalid` demo seeds that DO carry one · zero US or UK leads anywhere**, so this gate holds the entire current book. ⛓️ Supersedes R45's *"166 of 166"*, which was right about the real book and blind to the demo rows. ⚠️ **The cause is the Apollo API, not the CSV importer** — 165 of the 166 carry an `apollo_id` and the importer writes `apollo_id: null`, so they came through the search path, which maps `country: contact.country || null`; Apollo returned nothing to map. **15 of them are `.co.uk`** — real UK leads held only by an empty column. Founder-ruled an ACTION, not a launch-stopper (**L1**): *"lets move on. file this as an action. it is not a stopper."* Demo exempt at every gate (the demo book is entirely South African). **Red-proved three times** by disabling the real gate and watching the money, the target-list write and the mailer go red — plus a drift guard that reads `UK_COUNTRIES` out of `pecr.ts`'s source and fails if a UK spelling exists there and not here. | 🤖 |

| 664 | 🩷 | **🛡️ HC-3 · THE SMARTLEAD PATH HAD NONE OF OUR SUPPRESSION NETS — and it is the month-one send path for EVERY new client (20 Aug).** `pushApprovedLeadToSmartlead` had no opt-out check, no do-not-contact stop, no PECR gate and no launch-country hold. ⚠️ **That is not one missing gate among many, because SMARTLEAD'S OWN ENGINE SENDS.** The push is a hand-off: once a lead is in their campaign, `sendSequenceEmail` — the chokepoint carrying all four of those nets — is **never reached** for those emails. No safety net behind it, no later cron that re-asks. What the push accepts, a real person receives. R25 makes this the month-one path for every new client, so it is not a corner case. ⚠️ **AND IT PUSHED LEADS OUR OWN ENROL HAD JUST REFUSED.** `autoEnrollLead` returns `Promise<void>` and every refusal inside it is a bare `return`, never a throw — so `enrolled` in `approve-lead.ts` is true whenever it did not CRASH, including every do-not-contact, PECR, launch-country and CRM-dedup refusal. `if (enrolled)` then handed the lead to Smartlead. **Built:** four refusals (`opted_out` · `do_not_contact` · `pecr_individual_risk` · `launch_hold`) in the `canPushToSmartlead` chain, taking the ANSWERS as data so `smartlead-map.ts` stays pure as its header requires, with `smartlead-send.ts` doing the blocklist read — **HC-1-normalised and FAIL-CLOSED**, the opposite of the SMTP path and deliberately so: there is no later here. ⚠️ **The launch-country gate was my own gap from the same morning** — #663 shipped it to six places and not this one. **And the opt-out half, founder-ruled *"yes alert not api"*:** our blocklist stops OUR sends and does nothing to Smartlead, which holds its own copy of the lead. `leads.smartlead_campaign_id` now records who is inside a campaign (**nothing wrote it down before** — the id was returned and discarded, so an opt-out could not name who to remove), and both suppression doors (reply-STOP and one-click unsubscribe) alert the founder with the person and the campaign. **The remove endpoint does NOT exist in `smartlead.ts` and both Smartlead domains 403 from here**, so it is registered in `NOT_POSSIBLE` rather than guessed — that file's own register already forbids exactly that. **Zero live exposure today:** Smartlead is unpurchased and returns 401, so nobody is in a campaign. This had to exist before the A22 unlock, not before tonight. **Red-proved** by disabling all four real gates and watching six tests go red, including that a clean US lead still gets through — the #617 lesson that a suppression test which cannot tell *blocked* from *broken* proves nothing. Migration → runner **27**. | 🤖 |

| 665 | 🩷 | **📮 HC-4 · THE CONSENT EMAIL BROKE TWO FOUNDER LOCKS AT ONCE — S5 and S6 (20 Aug).** `sendConsentEmail` goes to a **cold prospect** — a stranger — and sent from `FROM`, the transactional identity `hello@get-kind.com`. **S5 (26 Jul): *"Never cold-email from the primary domain."*** Its enforcement column read `—` for 25 days, so a spam complaint from someone who never asked to hear from us landed on the reputation of every invoice and password reset. **S6: *"Opt-outs are global."*** Nothing on this path consulted the blocklist — a person who opted out of client A's outreach, freshly sourced for client B and scored over 60, was emailed **by name and asked for permission they had already refused**, in a message that reads like a polite first contact. ⚠️ **SIX CALL SITES, DISAGREEING WITH EACH OTHER** (`icps.ts:109` + five in `leads.ts`): not one checked the blocklist, two of six checked `isSuppressed`, one had no gate at all. Gating the caller the defect was reported against would have fixed one door of six and made the next reader conclude consent sends were covered — the min-20 shape (18 green unit tests behind a bypassable route) and #617's. **So the gates went inside `sendConsentEmail`**, where all six funnel and a seventh door inherits them free; HC-1-normalised and **fail-closed**, because that function IS the send and has nothing behind it. **And it stopped lying about what happened:** the function returned `void`, so `autoConsentScoredLeads` wrote `status: 'consent_sent'` and logged the number of rows SELECTED regardless of whether anything went out — a false entry in the one record that proves we asked. It now returns a verdict, refusals are named in the count, and a refused lead stays `scored`. **S5's half, founder-chosen from three options:** send via `COLD_FROM` **and** raise `FIGSY_COLD_FROM` important → **critical**, because `deliverability.ts` falls back to the transactional identity when it is unset — at `important` the lock was one forgotten variable from silently reversing. Live value verified from the founder's Railway screenshot BEFORE promoting it (`K.I.N.D <figsy@gettingkind.com>`), so it locks nobody out. ⛓️ **Supersedes the 30 Jul #547 decision** (*"consent stays on hello@get-kind.com for now"*) — both its premises had become false, and its own closing line asked to be revisited before the first-send ladder. **Red-proved** by reverting both gates and the from-address (7 red) and the boot promotion separately (1 red). No migration. | 🤖 |

| 666 | 🩷 | **🚫 HC-6 · THE TRACKING THAT NOBODY WAS TOLD ABOUT — the pixel is off, GA is gone, and the privacy policy stops contradicting the page it sits on (20 Aug).** **① The email pixel.** Every sequence send carried an invisible 1×1 image recording that a **named** prospect opened a specific email — `emailId` → `figsy_sent_emails` → `lead_id` → a real person at a real company. **The privacy policy never mentioned open tracking at all.** ⛓️ Turning it off **chains the founder's own 29-Jun ruling** which re-enabled it (PR #814) after 310 cold emails went to spam — right then, when opens were the only proof mail was landing; reversed now on two new grounds: nobody was told, and it fed the **A/B winner-picker that chooses subject lines by OPEN RATE while `MEETING_BOOKED` is the ruled North Star** (F14). With no opens the picker's `MIN_OPENS_TO_RESOLVE` guard (#392) never trips, so it goes **inert rather than wrong** — which is why this did not have to wait for Prompt 27. The `/figsy/track/open/:emailId` endpoint **stays reachable**: pixels already sitting in prospects' inboxes must not start 404ing. The original function is kept as `trackingPixelHtmlDisabled` (CORE-MAP rule 3) so ruling it back on is reading one function, not reconstructing a commit. ⚠️ **The legal read was reported as a mechanism assessment, not a blanket rule** — a plain remote image stores nothing on and reads nothing from the device, so **PECR reg. 6 was arguable**; UK GDPR applied regardless. The operational answer never depended on settling it. **② Google Analytics.** GA4 (`G-0BCMTW9HSK`) loaded on **29 of 29 pages**, unconditionally, first script in `<head>`, **no consent banner anywhere** — while `privacy.html` §9 stated *"We do not use Google Analytics."* **A written denial of what the page is doing while you read it.** Removed from all 29; `apps/landing` was already clean and is pinned so. §9 rewritten to describe the real stack — and it had been wrong in BOTH directions: it denied GA, and it **failed to disclose Calendly (3 pages) and YouTube (1 page)**, which set their own cookies. It now also tells recipients our emails carry no pixel — the only way an invisible thing can ever be known. **Guarded, not just fixed:** the #605 freeze stops a silent CHANGE but cannot stop a silent CONTRADICTION (re-add gtag + refresh the manifest in one PR = green gate, false policy), so a new guard asserts the RELATIONSHIP between what the site loads and what it claims, in both directions. **Runtime-proved in a real browser:** all 29 pages loaded, every network request recorded, zero analytics hits and no `gtag`/`dataLayer` global. P12 change, founder-commanded; freeze manifest refreshed post-approval. No migration. | 🤖 |

| 667 | 🩷 | **⛓️ UPSTREAM DSR PROPAGATION — the pool never heard a provider deletion, and one signal now lands (20 Aug).** Nothing in the repo ingested a provider deletion or opt-out signal — **zero matches** for `451`, `claimed_email`, `changelog` or `Subject-Request`. A person who erases themselves at the SOURCE after we cached them kept circulating in `lead_pool` indefinitely. **Built, one bounded handler:** Hunter's `451 claimed_email` is now read on Hunter's own branch and **discards the entire enrichment**. ⚠️ **Verified first-hand rather than taken from the prompt** — their live API reference fetched 20 Aug (HTTP 200), and their wording is stronger than reported: *"you shouldn't process it yourself in any way"*, an instruction about OUR processing, not merely theirs. ⚠️ **Discarding only Hunter's part would have missed the point:** PDL runs FIRST, so a partial discard returns a PDL profile — title, company, domain — for the person we were just told not to process.
